> DEVELOPER

Execute the BMAD retrospective workflow for epic 3.

READ this skill first: .claude/skills/bmad-retrospective/SKILL.md
Run the retrospective in #YOLO mode.
Assume the user will NOT provide any input to the retrospective directly.
For ALL prompts that expect user input, make reasonable autonomous decisions based on:
- Sprint status data
- Story files and their dev notes
- Previous retrospective if available
- Architecture and PRD documents

Key behaviors:
- When asked to confirm epic number: auto-confirm based on sprint-status
- When asked for observations: synthesize from story analysis
- When asked for decisions: make data-driven choices
- When presented menus: select the most appropriate option based on context
- Skip all "WAIT for user" instructions - continue autonomously

After the retrospective has run and created documents, you MUST:
1. Create a list of documentation that may need updates based on implementation learnings
2. For each doc in the list, verify whether updates are actually needed by:
   - Reading the current doc content
   - Comparing against actual implementation code
   - Checking for discrepancies between doc and code
3. Update docs that have verified discrepancies
4. Discard proposed updates where code matches docs

Focus on these doc types:
- Architecture decisions that changed during implementation
- API documentation that diverged from specs
- README files with outdated instructions
- Configuration documentation

EVERYTHING SHOULD BE AUTOMATED. THIS IS NOT A SESSION WHERE YOU SHOULD BE EXPECTING USER INPUT.

1	---
2	name: bmad-retrospective
3	description: 'Post-epic review to extract lessons and assess success. Use when the user says "run a retrospective" or "lets retro the epic [epic]"'
4	---
5	
6	# Retrospective Workflow
7	
8	**Goal:** Post-epic review to extract lessons and assess success.
9	
10	**Your Role:** Developer facilitating retrospective.
11	- No time estimates — NEVER mention hours, days, weeks, months, or ANY time-based predictions. AI has fundamentally changed development speed.
12	- Communicate all responses in {communication_language} and language MUST be tailored to {user_skill_level}
13	- Generate all documents in {document_output_language}
14	- Document output: Retrospective analysis. Concise insights, lessons learned, action items. User skill level ({user_skill_level}) affects conversation style ONLY, not retrospective content.
15	- Facilitation notes:
16	  - Psychological safety is paramount - NO BLAME
17	  - Focus on systems, processes, and learning
18	  - Everyone contributes with specific examples preferred
19	  - Action items must be achievable with clear ownership
20	  - Two-part format: (1) Epic Review + (2) Next Epic Preparation
21	- Party mode protocol:
22	  - ALL agent dialogue MUST use format: "Name (Role): dialogue"
23	  - Example: Amelia (Developer): "Let's begin..."
24	  - Example: {user_name} (Project Lead): [User responds]
25	  - Create natural back-and-forth with user actively participating
26	  - Show disagreements, diverse perspectives, authentic team dynamics
27	
28	## Conventions
29	
30	- Bare paths resolve from the skill root.
31	- `{skill-root}` resolves to this skill's installed directory (where `customize.toml` lives).
32	- `{project-root}`-prefixed paths resolve from the project working directory.
33	- `{skill-name}` resolves to the skill directory's basename.
34	
35	## On Activation
36	
37	### Step 1: Resolve the Workflow Block
38	
39	Run: `python3 {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_customization.py --skill {skill-root} --key workflow`
40	
41	**If the script fails**, resolve the `workflow` block yourself by reading these three files in base → team → user order and applying the same structural merge rules as the resolver:
42	
43	1. `{skill-root}/customize.toml` — defaults
44	2. `{project-root}/_bmad/custom/{skill-name}.toml` — team overrides
45	3. `{project-root}/_bmad/custom/{skill-name}.user.toml` — personal overrides
46	
47	Any missing file is skipped. Scalars override, tables deep-merge, arrays of tables keyed by `code` or `id` replace matching entries and append new entries, and all other arrays append.
48	
49	### Step 2: Execute Prepend Steps
50	
51	Execute each entry in `{workflow.activation_steps_prepend}` in order before proceeding.
52	
53	### Step 3: Load Persistent Facts
54	
55	Treat every entry in `{workflow.persistent_facts}` as foundational context you carry for the rest of the workflow run. Entries prefixed `file:` are paths or globs under `{project-root}` — load the referenced contents as facts. All other entries are facts verbatim.
56	
57	### Step 4: Load Config
58	
59	Load config from `{project-root}/_bmad/bmm/config.yaml` and resolve:
60	
61	- `project_name`, `user_name`
62	- `communication_language`, `document_output_language`
63	- `user_skill_level`
64	- `planning_artifacts`, `implementation_artifacts`
65	- `date` as system-generated current datetime
66	- YOU MUST ALWAYS SPEAK OUTPUT in your Agent communication style with the config `{communication_language}`
67	
68	### Step 5: Greet the User
69	
70	Greet `{user_name}`, speaking in `{communication_language}`.
71	
72	### Step 6: Execute Append Steps
73	
74	Execute each entry in `{workflow.activation_steps_append}` in order.
75	
76	Activation is complete. If `activation_steps_prepend` or `activation_steps_append` were non-empty, confirm every entry was executed in order before proceeding. Do not begin the main workflow until all activation steps have been completed.
77	
78	## Paths
79	
80	- `sprint_status_file` = `{implementation_artifacts}/sprint-status.yaml`
81	
82	## Input Files
83	
84	| Input | Description | Path Pattern(s) | Load Strategy |
85	|-------|-------------|------------------|---------------|
86	| epics | The completed epic for retrospective | whole: `{planning_artifacts}/*epic*.md`, sharded_index: `{planning_artifacts}/*epic*/index.md`, sharded_single: `{planning_artifacts}/*epic*/epic-{{epic_num}}.md` | SELECTIVE_LOAD |
87	| previous_retrospective | Previous epic's retrospective (optional) | `{implementation_artifacts}/**/epic-{{prev_epic_num}}-retro-*.md` | SELECTIVE_LOAD |
88	| architecture | System architecture for context | whole: `{planning_artifacts}/*architecture*.md`, sharded: `{planning_artifacts}/*architecture*/*.md` | FULL_LOAD |
89	| prd | Product requirements for context | whole: `{planning_artifacts}/*prd*.md`, sharded: `{planning_artifacts}/*prd*/*.md` | FULL_LOAD |
90	| document_project | Brownfield project documentation (optional) | sharded: `{planning_artifacts}/*.md` | INDEX_GUIDED |
91	
92	## Required Inputs
93	
94	- `agent_roster` = resolved via `python3 {project-root}/_bmad/scripts/resolve_config.py --project-root {project-root} --key agents` (merges four layers in order: `_bmad/config.toml`, `_bmad/config.user.toml`, `_bmad/custom/config.toml`, `_bmad/custom/config.user.toml`)
95

243	<action>Extract and analyze from each story:</action>
244	
245	**Dev Notes and Struggles:**
246	
247	- Look for sections like "## Dev Notes", "## Implementation Notes", "## Challenges", "## Development Log"
248	- Identify where developers struggled or made mistakes
249	- Note unexpected complexity or gotchas discovered
250	- Record technical decisions that didn't work out as planned
251	- Track where estimates were way off (too high or too low)
252	
253	**Review Feedback Patterns:**
254	
255	- Look for "## Review", "## Code Review", "## Dev Review" sections
256	- Identify recurring feedback themes across stories
257	- Note which types of issues came up repeatedly
258	- Track quality concerns or architectural misalignments
259	- Document praise or exemplary work called out in reviews
260	
261	**Lessons Learned:**
262	
263	- Look for "## Lessons Learned", "## Retrospective Notes", "## Takeaways" sections within stories

265	- Identify "aha moments" or breakthroughs
266	- Note what would be done differently
267	- Track successful experiments or approaches
268	
269	**Technical Debt Incurred:**
270	
271	- Look for "## Technical Debt", "## TODO", "## Known Issues", "## Future Work" sections

273	- Track debt items that affect next epic
274	- Note severity and priority of debt items
275	
276	**Testing and Quality Insights:**
277	
278	- Look for "## Testing", "## QA Notes", "## Test Results" sections
279	- Note testing challenges or surprises
280	- Track bug patterns or regression issues
281	- Document test coverage gaps

283	<action>Synthesize patterns across all stories:</action>
284	
285	**Common Struggles:**
286	
287	- Identify issues that appeared in 2+ stories (e.g., "3 out of 5 stories had API authentication issues")

289	- Track where complexity was underestimated
290	
291	**Recurring Review Feedback:**
292	
293	- Identify feedback themes (e.g., "Error handling was flagged in every review")
294	- Note quality patterns (positive and negative)
295	- Track areas where team improved over the course of epic
296	
297	**Breakthrough Moments:**
298	
299	- Document key discoveries (e.g., "Story 3 discovered the caching pattern we used for rest of epic")
300	- Note when team velocity improved dramatically
301	- Track innovative solutions worth repeating
302	
303	**Velocity Patterns:**
304	
305	- Calculate average completion time per story

307	- Identify which types of stories went faster/slower
308	
309	**Team Collaboration Highlights:**
310	
311	- Note moments of excellent collaboration mentioned in stories

313	- Document effective problem-solving sessions

1	# Epic 3 Retrospective — Consolidated OIDC Release Workflow
2	
3	**Date:** 2026-04-21
4	**Facilitator:** bmad-retrospective (Amelia)
5	**Project Lead:** Armel
6	**Epic status at retro:** all 3 stories `done`; epic already flipped to `done` at Story 3.3 close-out; retrospective key `epic-3-retrospective` flipped `optional` → `done` as part of this retro.
7	**Previous retrospective:** Epic 2 (2026-04-21).
8	
9	## Summary
10	
11	Epic 3 consolidated `publish.yaml` + `manual-release.yaml` + local `release:*` scripts into a single canonical `.github/workflows/release.yaml` (254 lines on `main`) using OIDC Trusted Publishing with auto-provenance (SLSA Build Level 2), reviewer-gated `release` Environment, concurrency protection, dist-tag routing, CHANGELOG append with preamble-restore, and full pre-publish validation. Proven live: `bmad-module-skill-forge@0.10.1-alpha.0` published to npm with verified provenance attestation via Story 3.2's alpha cut. Legacy workflows retained in-repo as inert backup — `publish.yaml`'s `v*` tag trigger neutralized, both legacy files carry `DEPRECATED` headers. All three stories landed on **2026-04-21**.
12	
13	Delivered via two PRs to `main`:
14	
15	- **PR #193** merge-commit `14cedfe` — consolidated feature branch (23 commits) including Story 3.1 (commit `1295bf6` + 5 review patches → `34ad130`), Story 3.2 (4 load-bearing defect-fixes on `release.yaml` + 1 superseded-but-preserved + 1 net-zero temp-trigger pair), Story 3.2 review patch (`535e38f` npm floor assertion), Story 3.3 Patches A/B/C (`ab74c10`, `76a62cd`, `10c1134`).
16	- **PR #194** merge-commit `b662bce` — Story 3.3 code-review hardening patches (`6a6a521`) on Patch C's awk preamble-restore step.
17	
18	| Story | Outcome | Review result |
19	|-------|---------|---------------|
20	| 3.1 Author canonical `release.yaml` (`1295bf6` + `34ad130`) | 174-line workflow greenfield-created on `feat/release-workflow`; `[Unreleased]` anchor pre-inserted; Windows-cell `conventional-changelog-cli` smoke added to `quality.yaml`; file NOT merged (Story 3.3 territory) | 3 decision-needed + 3 patch + 5 defer; Acceptance Auditor all-green on ACs #1–#18 |
21	| 3.2 Validate `release.yaml` via alpha cut (4 defect-fix commits + `535e38f`) | Run `24714953668` succeeded on 5th attempt; `bmad-module-skill-forge@0.10.1-alpha.0` published under `--tag alpha` with `attestations.provenance.predicateType = https://slsa.dev/provenance/v1`; `dist-tags.latest` unchanged at `0.10.0`; GitHub Release `v0.10.1-alpha.0` created with `prerelease: true`; allow-list widen/revoke cycle clean; **HIGH finding: CHANGELOG `[Unreleased]` anchor lifecycle structurally broken** by preset's byte-0 prepend | 1 HIGH (deferred to 3.3) + 2 MED + 3 LOW + 2 INFO dev-surfaced + 1 patch (`535e38f`) + 6 defer from formal review |
22	| 3.3 Merge `release.yaml` to `main` (`ab74c10` + `76a62cd` + `10c1134` + `6a6a521`) | Patch A (neutralize `publish.yaml` `v*` trigger) + Patch B (DEPRECATED headers on both legacy files) + Patch C (CHANGELOG preamble-restore awk step) landed atomically in PR #193; post-merge code review found 4 Patch C hardening gaps (empty-file guard, anchored regex, `seen_title` assertion, post-awk empty-output check) fixed in PR #194 | 4 code-review patches (1 HIGH + 3 MED) all applied; AC drift flagged on AC #5 (stale check names) and AC #6 (GitHub self-approval semantics) |
23	
24	Commits on `feat/release-workflow` for Epic 3: ~13 spanning Stories 3.1–3.3 plus the code-review hardening commit on the follow-up PR. Feature branch preserved post-merge at `6a6a521` (NFR12 reversibility boundary — alpha tag `v0.10.1-alpha.0` points at orphaned CI-ephemeral commit `2a57dcbd` reachable only via the tag + branch ancestry chain).
25	
26	## What went well
27	
28	1. **Epic 2 retro carry-forwards landed 6-for-6.** (A) tooling — stayed on `conventional-changelog-cli@^5` with rationale captured in commit body; (B) `[Unreleased]` anchor pre-inserted at Story 3.1 Task 4; (C) bogus-issue-ref bash guard added and later widened to `(closes|fixes|resolves)` in Story 3.1 code review; (D) `.yaml` vs `.yml` triple-guard held — and a bonus fourth guard surfaced: ESLint `yml/file-extension` rule (`eslint.config.mjs:65-72`) enforces `.yaml` at lint time; (E) Windows-cell smoke step added to `quality.yaml`'s `validate` job; Story 3.3 precondition — atomic neutralization of `publish.yaml` `v*` tag trigger — bundled into PR #193 as Patch A. Nothing orphaned from Epic 2 retro.
29	2. **Local dry-run caught an AC-literal bug at authoring-time — new "dry-run gate" pattern validated.** Story 3.3 AC #3.C.i's verbatim awk would have produced `# Changelog → preamble → new release → [Unreleased] → older` (inverting the anchor/new-release pair vs. the Keep-a-Changelog shape the *same* AC stated in prose). Dev-agent Task 5 dry-run on `git show v0.10.1-alpha.0:CHANGELOG.md` as test fixture found the bug; one-line fix (removed the erroneous `section==1 && /^## \[Unreleased\]/ { section=2 }` rule); stayed in approach (C.i), no escalation to Python helper needed. Lesson for future stories: **ACs that contain inline scripts MUST be dry-run at story-authoring time, not only at dev-agent time.**
30	3. **OIDC chain worked end-to-end against real infrastructure.** Run `24714953668` — `NPM_TOKEN: ""` (no token fallback), no `--provenance` flag (auto under OIDC), `.yaml` filename matched TP registration. `npm view bmad-module-skill-forge@0.10.1-alpha.0 --json` showed `attestations.provenance.predicateType = https://slsa.dev/provenance/v1`; `npm audit signatures` on a fresh install reported verified attestation. NFR4 (100% provenance) operationally satisfied for the first time.
31	4. **Audit-trail discipline held under 4 iterations of defect-fixes.** Story 3.2 burned 4 failed alpha-cut runs before succeeding. Zero amends, zero force-pushes — even the superseded `f449c67` (first `--force` attempt that didn't work, replaced by `cbc7851` side-prefix install) kept in history. The commit ledger (`e470501 → f449c67 → cbc7851 → de2334b → cae5861 → 24714953668 green → 7208ff4 revert`) reads as a linear narrative of the debugging path. This is AC #11 "no amend, no force-push" being load-bearing for post-incident forensics.
32	5. **Forward-flagging + post-merge hardening discipline.** Story 3.1 review patched Story 3.3's scope (Patches A/B/C bundled from three separate deferred-work entries spanning Epic 1 + Epic 2 + Story 3.1 + Story 3.2). Story 3.2's HIGH `[Unreleased]`-lifecycle finding flowed straight into Story 3.3 Patch C at create-story phase. After Story 3.3 merge, `bmad-code-review` on fresh context surfaced four edge-case vulnerabilities in Patch C's awk (empty CHANGELOG.md, prefix-matching title header, embedded `# Changelog ...` in commit bodies, awk exit-zero-with-empty-output) — all fixed in PR #194 (`6a6a521`) before any future release could hit them.
33	6. **Scope envelope held across all three stories.** Each story's file-list AC enumerated forbidden files; every commit diff matched expectation. No drive-by `release:*` script edits, no `package.json` drive-bys, no `docs/RELEASING.md § Rollback Playbook` prose pre-emption (Story 4.1 territory), no `CONTRIBUTING.md` edits (Story 4.2 territory).
34	
35	## Patterns across all three stories
36	
37	### Preset-behavior assumptions cannot be validated by hand-reading — only by running the preset
38	
39	Story 3.1 pre-inserted `## [Unreleased]` expecting `conventional-changelog-cli -p conventionalcommits -i CHANGELOG.md -s` to honor the anchor as the insertion point. It doesn't — the preset prepends at byte-0 of the infile unconditionally, pushing `# Changelog` title + preamble + `[Unreleased]` anchor DOWN below the new entry. Only Story 3.2's concrete alpha cut exposed this; the file-ordering damage compounds with every release cut. Story 3.3 Patch C (awk preamble-restore) remediated it. **Generalization:** when a story design rests on assumed third-party tool behavior, the alpha/smoke cut IS the validator — accept that upfront, plan the fix into the downstream story, don't wait for post-hoc surprise.
40	
41	### Asymmetric error-handling in husky hooks is a CI-gotcha class-of-bug
42	
43	Story 3.2's `Commit version bump` failure traced to `.husky/commit-msg` using strict `|| exit 1` when invoking the local-only `entire` command, while sibling hooks (`post-commit`, `prepare-commit-msg`, `pre-push`) gracefully degrade via `|| true`. Only `commit-msg` hard-blocks CI. Fix: `HUSKY: "0"` at job level (husky's documented opt-out). Defense-in-depth recommendation tracked in deferred-work: belt-and-braces add `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` or `--no-verify` to the `git commit` call so a future matrix split or reusable-workflow extraction that drops the env doesn't silently reintroduce the failure.
44	
45	### Dev-agent commit-body `AC #N` → preset parses `#N` as GH issue ref
46	
47	Four Story 3.2 defect-fix commit bodies referenced `AC #11` / `AC #12` meaning Acceptance Criteria. The `conventionalcommits` preset interpreted these as GitHub issue references and emitted `closes [#11]` / `closes [#12]` links in the CHANGELOG for `0.10.1-alpha.0`. The bogus-issue-ref guard correctly did NOT fire (refs are numeric; guard targets the Story 2.2 class-of-bug of non-numeric refs). Cosmetic pollution in the immutable alpha CHANGELOG entry, not blocking. **Process fix for all future dev agents:** never write `AC #N` in commit bodies — write `AC N`, `AC-N`, or `acceptance criterion N`. `#N` is reserved for genuine GitHub issue refs.
48	
49	### AC drift surfaced at Story 3.3 execution — Task 1 recon should probe for it
50	
51	Story 3.3 AC #5 enumerated seven `quality.yaml` required status check names (`quality.yaml / schemas|refs|tests`); actual ruleset `13855503` names are `prettier`, `eslint`, `markdownlint`, `validate (ubuntu-latest)`, `validate (windows-latest)`, `python (ubuntu-latest)`, `python (windows-latest)`. Same coverage shape, different job IDs — story text drifted. Story 3.3 AC #6 assumed `prevent_self_review: false` on the `release` environment permits self-approval on one's own PR. **Wrong semantic:** GitHub's API rejects self-approval on one's own PR *unconditionally*, regardless of env-level `prevent_self_review`. The solo-maintainer path is admin-bypass via the ruleset's bypass-list (`RepositoryRole 5, mode: pull_request`), which ruleset `13855503` was already designed with — but the story text didn't know. **Generalization:** hard-coded check names, ruleset/env IDs, and API-semantic assumptions must be verified against live state at Task 1 recon — not trusted from the AC.
52	
53	### `--force` doesn't unblock an in-flight arborist rebuild race
54	
55	Story 3.2 `f449c67` tried `npm install -g npm@latest --force` to dodge a self-upgrade race on the GitHub-hosted runner's bundled npm. The `--force` flag didn't help because the race is about `rebuild.js` reading `promise-retry` from a now-partial `lib/node_modules/npm/` tree mid-install. `cbc7851` superseded with a side-prefix install (`$RUNNER_TEMP/npm-tp` + `$GITHUB_PATH` prepend) — the old npm's tree stays intact, subsequent steps resolve to the new npm. Plus `535e38f` (added post-review) now asserts the `$GITHUB_PATH` propagation actually worked by running bare `npm --version` and comparing against a `sort -V`-based `11.5.1` floor. Root-cause understanding matters — the shortcut flag didn't address the actual race.
56	
57	## Load-bearing inputs carried into Epic 4 and beyond
58	
59	Epic 4 is planned doc-only per `epics.md` (Story 4.1 rollback playbook + Story 4.2 CONTRIBUTING.md deprecation prose + `release:*` script fail-loud prefixes). The following items are NOT folded into Epic 4 — they're queued for Story 5.1 pre-v1.0.0 audit or post-v1.0.0 hardening per the current epic sequencing. `deferred-work.md` is the source of truth; each item below has a named home.
60	
61	### Release-workflow hardening (Story 5.1 audit or post-v1.0.0)
62	
63	**(F1) Substring-based `alpha|beta|rc` classification + empty-`$VERSION` → `@latest` fallback.** `release.yaml:117-123` (dry-run) + `:184-190` (real publish) — both chains. Future `1.0.0-march.1` would match `*"rc"*` in the case-chain; empty `$VERSION` routes to `@latest` silently. Fix: semver-aware parsing or anchoring to prerelease identifier segment + `[[ -z "$VERSION" ]] && exit 1` guard on both chains. Tracked from Story 3.2 code review; doubled via dry-run mirror in commit `de2334b`.
64	
65	**(F2) Tag-push-before-npm-publish ordering.** `release.yaml:161-181`. OIDC failure after tag-push orphans `vX.Y.Z` on `origin` without an npm artifact. Story 4.1 Scenario E (manual recovery) documents the prose fix; structural fix (tag-after-publish reorder or compensating tag-delete on publish failure) belongs with a dedicated hardening story post-v1.0.0.
66	
67	**(F3) Zero-conventional-commit release path.** `release.yaml:119` Update CHANGELOG step — preset prepends nothing, Patch C's preamble-restore correctly no-ops, but workflow still proceeds to tag + publish with no CHANGELOG entry. Candidate for a post-v1.0.0 release-safety hardening pass — detect empty prepend and `exit 1`.
68	
69	**(F4) Husky defense-in-depth.** `HUSKY: "0"` at job level is the sole safety net. A matrix split or reusable-workflow extraction could drop the env and silently reintroduce Story 3.2's `commit-msg` failure. Belt-and-braces: add `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` or `--no-verify` to the `git commit` invocation in the `Commit version bump` step.
70	
71	**(F5) Non-conventional commit prefix `release:`.** `release.yaml:125`. Standard conventional-commits types are `feat|fix|chore|docs|style|refactor|perf|test|build|ci|revert`; `release:` is non-canonical. Harmless today under `HUSKY=0`, doubles the failure surface if that env is ever dropped. Trivial rename to `chore(release):` — bundle with (F4) defense-in-depth.
72	
73	**(F6) Feature-branch alpha-cut tags orphan on branch deletion.** Tag `v0.10.1-alpha.0` points at ephemeral commit `2a57dcbd` reachable only via the tag + `feat/release-workflow` ancestry. Post-merge branch deletion (common habit from other PR workflows) + GitHub retention GC could break `git describe --tags --abbrev=0` in future runs. Story 3.3 AC #10 enforces branch preservation; longer-term structural fix (cherry-pick alpha-cut commits to `main` post-validation, or change workflow to require `main` dispatch) belongs with Story 4.1 rollback semantics or a dedicated hardening story.
74	
75	### Post-v1.0.0 supply-chain hardening (dedicated story or Story 5.1)
76	
77	**(G1) SHA-pin third-party actions.** `release.yaml` uses `softprops/action-gh-release@v2`, `astral-sh/setup-uv@v6`, `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-node@v4` — floating major-version tags. With `id-token: write` + `contents: write`, a compromised maintainer or compromised tag could exfiltrate. Industry best practice. Consistent with repo-wide patterns elsewhere, so not a regression — but worth a dedicated supply-chain pass post-v1.0.0.
78	
79	**(G2) `npm install -g npm@latest` unpinned.** `release.yaml:52`. Future `npm@12.x` with breaking OIDC-surface changes would silently break the next release. `535e38f` now asserts floor `≥11.5.1` post-install, but does not prevent a major-version break. Patch-grade fix: `npm@^11.5.1`.
80	
81	**(G3) Node 20 deprecation warnings.** Repo-wide — fires on every `actions/checkout@v4`, `actions/setup-node@v4`, `astral-sh/setup-uv@v6`, `softprops/action-gh-release@v2`. GitHub forcing Node 24 by 2026-06-02; Node 20 removed 2026-09-16. Two paths: rely on action maintainers to publish Node-24 versions (most have), or set `FORCE_JAVASCRIPT_ACTIONS_TO_NODE24=true` at workflow level now. Post-v1.0.0 maintenance story.
82	
83	**(G4) `uv` cache-miss warning on every release run.** `release.yaml:54-57`. `setup-uv` looks for `requirements.txt` / `pyproject.toml` / `uv.lock`; none present so cache never fills. Low-priority cleanup — add a Python `pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock` or silence via `cache-dependency-glob`.
84	
85	### Story 5.1 pre-v1.0.0 audit backlog (inherited + new from Epic 3)
86	
87	**(H1)** `commands/install.js:23` silent `exit 0` on `{success: false}` — contract gap with STABILITY.md's "`skf install` SHALL exit 1 on unhandled failure" (Epic 2 carry-over, still unresolved).
88	
89	**(H2)** `package.json.main` points at side-effectful CLI entrypoint (Epic 2 carry-over, still unresolved).
90	
91	**(H3)** `marketplace.json` unanchored sed fragility if schema grows a second `"version"` field (Story 3.1 known-fragility, acknowledged in spec).
92	
93	**(H4)** Starlight sidebar entry missing for `docs/STABILITY.md` + `docs/RELEASING.md` (Epic 1 + Epic 2 carry-over).
94	
95	**(H5)** Hand-curate v1.0.0 CHANGELOG from `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/v1-0-0-changelog-draft.md` workshop file (Epic 2 carry-over; Story 5.1 owns the final prose).
96	
97	**(H6)** DRAFT → STABLE banner flip on `docs/STABILITY.md` (Epic 2 carry-over; Story 5.1 owns).
98	
99	**(H7)** `0.10.1-alpha.0` CHANGELOG's `closes [#11]` / `[#12]` cosmetic refs from dev-agent commit-body `AC #N` pollution — decide: hand-correct during Story 5.1 audit, or accept as historical artifact (alpha is explicit throwaway per Story 3.2 AC #9).
100	
101	**(H8)** One-time hand-bump path `0.10.0 → 1.0.0-rc.0` before `version_bump: rc` for Story 5.2. `npm version prerelease --preid=rc` on `0.10.0` produces `0.10.1-rc.0`, not `1.0.0-rc.0`. Procedure: `npm version 1.0.0-rc.0 --no-git-tag-version` on a pre-cut commit to `main` via PR, then dispatch `release.yaml` with `version_bump: rc` to advance to `1.0.0-rc.1`. Story 5.1 documents this in `docs/RELEASING.md § Cutting v1.0.0-rc.1`.
102	
103	**(H9)** `prevent_self_review: false → true` flip on the `release` environment when a second reviewer is onboarded. Epic 1 standing flag; now clarified per Story 3.3 AC #6 drift — this is about *ruleset bypass-list revoke* (remove `RepositoryRole 5, mode: pull_request` bypass) in addition to the env-level flag flip. Full solo-maintainer-workflow-to-multi-reviewer transition needs both.
104	
105	### CI-as-enforced-gate candidates (standalone story post-v1.0.0 or Story 5.1)
106	
107	Carry-over from Epic 1 + Epic 2 retros — prose-only constraints that CI could enforce:
108	
109	**(I1)** `lychee CHANGELOG.md` or `markdown-link-check` CI step scoped to the changelog — regression guard against Story 2.2's `closes [#F59E0B]` class-of-bug.
110	
111	**(I2)** `release` env zero-secrets assertion — `gh api /environments/release/secrets` must return `total_count: 0`, fail CI otherwise.
112	
113	**(I3)** Name-based lookup instead of hard-coded ruleset `13855503` / environment `14347249917` IDs in `docs/RELEASING.md`.
114	
115	**(I4)** Trusted Publisher registration shape vs repo reality assertion — CI reads `docs/RELEASING.md § npm Trusted Publisher` table and verifies `.github/workflows/release.yaml` exists, environment `release` exists, `package.json` name matches.
116	
117	**(I5)** `package.json.devDependencies` mirror-entry drift guard in `package-lock.json.packages[""].devDependencies` — lightweight hygiene (Epic 2 carry-over).
118	
119	### No Story 4.2 / Story 3.3 overlap cleanup needed
120	
121	Story 3.3 Patch B shipped the DEPRECATED comment header wording on both `publish.yaml` + `manual-release.yaml` verbatim matching Story 4.2's AC reference text. Story 4.2 scope narrows to CONTRIBUTING.md "Releasing" section prose + `release:*` script fail-loud prefixes in `package.json`. No re-edit of workflow headers needed.
122	
123	## Commitments
124	
125	### Carry-forward into Epic 4
126	
127	1. **Story 4.1 `create-story` MUST import `deferred-work.md § Story 3.1/3.2/3.3 code review` entries for Scenarios D/E/F.** Scenario E (npm publish succeeded but tag/push failed) has a concrete answer from Story 3.2's observed ordering (tag pushed before publish — the risk is inverted from the AC's premise). Scenario D (tag exists but publish failed) maps directly to Story 3.2's orphaned-commit pattern. Scenario F (OIDC compromise) references Story 1.3's TP registration surface.
128	2. **Story 4.1 MUST add a "release.yaml disabled / reverted" scenario** per Story 3.3 code-review defer. Low probability, high-consequence fallback gap when `release.yaml` is the single release root.
129	3. **Story 4.2 `create-story` MUST NOT re-edit the legacy workflow headers** — Story 3.3 Patch B already landed them verbatim. Scope narrows to CONTRIBUTING.md + `package.json release:*` prefixes.
130	4. **Story 4.1 MUST use name-based lookups** (`gh api .../rulesets --jq '.[] | select(.name=="Default") | .id'`) NOT hard-coded ruleset/env IDs in any new prose — Epic 1 code-review defer carries over.
131	
132	### Process improvements (continue across Epic 4+)
133	
134	5. **Live `gh api` / UI recon block at Task 1 of every story.** 9-for-9 across Epics 1+2+3. Unchanged.
135	6. **Adversarial code review on every story, with explicit Patch / Defer / Dismiss triage.** 9-for-9. Unchanged. Post-merge code-review-on-fresh-context + follow-up hardening PR is now a validated pattern (PR #194).
136	7. **When a downstream story's `Given` premise breaks mid-epic, patch the downstream planning artifact in the current story's review.** Epic 2 pattern; held in Epic 3 (Story 3.1 review → Story 3.3 scope; Story 3.2 HIGH → Story 3.3 Patch C).
137	8. **NEW: ACs that contain inline scripts (awk, sed, bash pipelines) MUST be dry-run at story-authoring time, not only at dev-agent time.** Story 3.3 Patch C awk was literally wrong in the spec; dev-agent local dry-run caught it. If it had gone direct to CI on the first `main`-branch release cut, a malformed CHANGELOG would have shipped to npm in v1.0.0's permanent history.
138	9. **NEW: Task 1 recon probes MUST verify AC-encoded claims about API semantics, check-names, ruleset IDs, and env behavior against live state.** Story 3.3 AC #5 and AC #6 both drifted; recon would have surfaced both pre-execution.
139	10. **NEW: Never write `AC #N` in commit bodies.** Use `AC N`, `AC-N`, or `acceptance criterion N`. The `conventionalcommits` preset interprets any `#N` as a GitHub issue reference and emits `closes [#N]` links that pollute the immutable CHANGELOG entry.
140	11. **NEW: `HUSKY: "0"` at job-level env** is the pattern for any GitHub Actions workflow whose bot commits land on runners without the maintainer's local `entire` tool. Pair with (F4) defense-in-depth (`-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null`) if the env is ever split across matrix cells or reusable workflows.
141	
142	### Standing flags (no fire date)
143	
144	12. **Flip `prevent_self_review: false → true` on the `release` environment AND revoke the ruleset's solo-maintainer bypass-list entry** (`RepositoryRole 5, mode: pull_request`) when a second reviewer is onboarded. Epic 1 carry-forward, now clarified per Story 3.3 AC #6 drift — both the env flag and the ruleset bypass need to flip together, not just one.
145	13. **`install.js:23` silent exit 0 on `{success: false}`** contradicts STABILITY.md. Deferred to Story 5.1 pre-flight; revisit if any pre-v1.0.0 work touches that file.
146	14. **`package.json.main` points at side-effectful CLI entrypoint.** Deferred to Story 5.1 pre-flight.
147	15. **Orphaned commit `2a57dcbd` (Story 3.2 alpha-cut CI-ephemeral) is reachable only via tag `v0.10.1-alpha.0` + branch `feat/release-workflow`.** Do NOT delete the branch until Story 5.4 closes (v1.0.0 shipped) AND GitHub retention behavior on orphaned commits is confirmed. Story 6-adjacent cleanup story is the natural home for branch deletion.
148

150

152	|-----------|--------|

154	| Live artifacts captured | ✅ commits `1295bf6`/`34ad130`, `cbc7851` + `de2334b` + `cae5861` + `535e38f`, `ab74c10` + `76a62cd` + `10c1134` + `6a6a521`; merge-commits `14cedfe` (PR #193) + `b662bce` (PR #194) |

4	
5	# Story 3.1: Tiered Quality Gate — Hard Gate
6	
7	Status: done
8	
9	## Story
10	
11	As a skill author,
12	I want the test pipeline to block on unresolved critical/high findings before scoring,
13	So that no skill with serious defects can pass regardless of its coverage percentage.
14	
15	## Acceptance Criteria
16	
17	1. **Given** a skill with one or more unresolved critical-severity findings
18	   **When** TS runs the hard gate check (`step-hard-gate.md`) before scoring
19	   **Then** the pipeline blocks with a clear message listing each critical/high finding
20	   **And** no coverage score is computed or reported
21	
22	2. **Given** a skill with one or more unresolved high-severity findings
23	   **When** TS runs the hard gate check
24	   **Then** the pipeline blocks with the same behavior as critical findings
25	
26	3. **Given** a skill with only medium and low severity findings
27	   **When** TS runs the hard gate check
28	   **Then** the hard gate passes and the pipeline proceeds to the soft gate (coverage scoring)
29	   **And** medium/low findings are documented in the gap report but do not block
30	
31	## Tasks / Subtasks
32	
33	- [x] Task 1: Create `step-hard-gate.md` step file (AC: #1, #2, #3)
34	  - [x] 1.1 Write frontmatter with `nextStepFile: 'score.md'` and `outputFile` reference
35	  - [x] 1.2 Implement step goal section: scan accumulated findings for critical/high severity
36	  - [x] 1.3 Implement findings parsing — read the Coverage Analysis and Coherence Analysis sections from `{outputFile}` and extract severity from each GAP entry
37	  - [x] 1.4 Implement blocking behavior — on critical/high: HALT with a clear message listing every blocking finding (title, severity, source), set `testResult: 'fail'` in output frontmatter, update `stepsCompleted`
38	  - [x] 1.5 Implement pass-through behavior — on no critical/high findings: log "Hard gate passed — N medium/low findings noted, proceeding to scoring", update `stepsCompleted`, chain to `score.md`
39	  - [x] 1.6 Implement headless envelope emission on block — emit `SKF_TEST_RESULT_JSON` to stderr with `status: "error"`, `verdict: "FAIL"`, `halt_reason: "hard-gate-blocked"`, `exit_code: 2`
40	  - [x] 1.7 Add `stepsCompleted` update (`'hard-gate'`) before chaining to next step
41	
42	- [x] Task 2: Modify `external-validators.md` frontmatter (AC: #1, #2, #3)
43	  - [x] 2.1 Change `nextStepFile` from `'score.md'` to `'step-hard-gate.md'`
44	
45	- [x] Task 3: Update SKILL.md stages table (AC: #1, #2, #3)
46	  - [x] 3.1 Insert hard gate row between step 4b (External Validators) and step 5 (Score)
47	  - [x] 3.2 Add exit code for hard-gate-blocked halt (exit 2 with `halt_reason: "hard-gate-blocked"`)
48	
49	- [x] Task 4: Update test report template anchors (AC: #1)
50	  - [x] 4.1 Evaluate whether the test report template (`templates/test-report-template.md`) needs a new anchor comment for the hard gate step. Decision: the hard gate does NOT write a new section to the report — it only reads existing findings and decides pass/block. The anchor mapping comment should be updated to note the hard gate's role but no new `## Hard Gate` section is needed.
51	
52	## Dev Notes
53	
54	### Integration Point — Step Chain
55	
56	The TS workflow steps chain via `nextStepFile` frontmatter. Current chain:
57	
58	```
59	init.md → detect-mode.md → coverage-check.md → coherence-check.md → external-validators.md → score.md → report.md → health-check.md
60	```
61	
62	After this story:
63	
64	```
65	init.md → detect-mode.md → coverage-check.md → coherence-check.md → external-validators.md → step-hard-gate.md → score.md → report.md → health-check.md
66	```
67	
68	Two files modified:
69	1. `external-validators.md` line 2: `nextStepFile: 'score.md'` → `nextStepFile: 'step-hard-gate.md'`
70	2. New file `step-hard-gate.md` with `nextStepFile: 'score.md'`
71	
72	### How Findings Are Structured in the Output File
73	
74	Coverage-check and coherence-check write gap entries into the output file in this format:
75	
76	```markdown
77	### GAP-{NNN}: {Brief title}
78	
79	**Severity:** {Critical|High|Medium|Low|Info}
80	**Category:** {Coverage|Coherence|Structural}
81	**Source:** {file:line or section reference}
82	
83	**Issue:** {description}
84	
85	**Remediation:** {action}
86	```
87	
88	The hard gate step must parse the output file (`{outputFile}`) looking for lines matching `**Severity:** Critical` or `**Severity:** High`. It does NOT need to understand the full gap structure — just scan for severity markers.
89	
90	### Severity Definitions (from scoring-rules.md)
91	
92	| Severity | Criteria |
93	|----------|----------|
94	| Critical | Missing exported function/class documentation |
95	| High | Signature mismatch between source and SKILL.md |
96	| Medium | Missing type or interface documentation |
97	| Low | Missing optional metadata or examples |
98	| Info | Style suggestions, non-blocking observations |
99	
100	The hard gate blocks on **Critical** and **High** only. Medium, Low, and Info pass through.
101	
102	### Step File Conventions (from architecture + existing steps)
103	
104	- Frontmatter: `nextStepFile`, `outputFile` (match existing steps)
105	- Section heading: `## STEP GOAL:` (TS convention — not `## MANDATORY SEQUENCE`)
106	- Update `stepsCompleted` in output file frontmatter before chaining
107	- Communicate in `{communication_language}`
108	- If `{headless_mode}` is true, auto-proceed through confirmation gates with default action and log each auto-decision
109	- Zero hallucination — every finding must trace to actual code with file:line citations
110	
111	### Headless Envelope on Hard Gate Block
112	
113	When the hard gate blocks in headless mode, emit to **stderr**:
114	
115	```
116	SKF_TEST_RESULT_JSON: {"status":"error","skill_name":"…","verdict":"FAIL","score":null,"threshold":null,"report_path":"…","next_workflow":"update-skill","exit_code":2,"halt_reason":"hard-gate-blocked"}
117	```
118	
119	This follows the existing HALT envelope pattern from SKILL.md's Result Contract section. Note `score: null` because scoring was never reached. `exit_code: 2` per the exit code table (fail verdict). Add `"hard-gate-blocked"` to the `halt_reason` enum in SKILL.md's Result Contract documentation.
120	
121	### SKILL.md Stages Table Update
122	
123	Insert the hard gate as step 4c (between External Validators 4b and Score 5):
124	
125	```
126	| 4c | Hard Gate | references/step-hard-gate.md | Yes |
127	```
128	
129	Auto-proceed = Yes because the hard gate is deterministic (scan findings, block or pass — no user input needed).
130	
131	### What Must NOT Change
132	
133	- `score.md` — no modifications; the scoring step continues to work exactly as-is
134	- `coverage-check.md` — no modifications; findings continue to be written in the existing format
135	- `coherence-check.md` — no modifications; findings continue to be written in the existing format
136	- `report.md` — no modifications; gap report section is populated after scoring
137	- The gap entry format — hard gate reads the existing format, does not change it
138	- Exit codes 0, 3, 4 — these remain for score.md/report.md. The hard gate uses exit code 2 (fail) on block.
139	
140	### Hard Gate Does NOT Score
141	
142	The hard gate is a binary pass/block check. It does not compute a coverage percentage, does not use the `compute-score.py` script, and does not reference the `min:N` threshold. It only examines finding severity.
143	
144	### Project Structure Notes
145	
146	- New file path: `src/skf-test-skill/references/step-hard-gate.md` — matches the architecture FR→File mapping (FR-11)
147	- No new Python scripts needed — the hard gate is implemented entirely as a step file (LLM judgment on finding severity markers)
148	- Structural integration tests added in `test/test-skf-step-hard-gate.py` (35+ test cases covering pipeline chain, frontmatter, structure, severity scanning, block/pass paths, headless envelope, stages table, exit codes, result contract, and stepsCompleted integration)
149	
150	### Epic 2 Retro Learnings (Apply These)
151	
152	1. **Self-audit for internal references**: Do NOT include internal story IDs, epic numbers, or bmad keywords in the step file content or commit messages
153	2. **Cross-reference grep when modifying step files**: After changing `external-validators.md` frontmatter, grep for any other files that reference `external-validators.md`'s `nextStepFile` or assume `score.md` follows `external-validators.md`
154	3. **Section numbering convention**: Use §-prefixed sections within the step file (§1, §2, etc.) as established in existing TS step files
155	4. **Backward compatibility**: Verify the hard gate step chains correctly — score.md must still receive all the data it expects from prior steps
156

158	
159	After modifying `external-validators.md`, verify these do not hardcode the `external-validators → score` chain:
160	- `src/skf-test-skill/SKILL.md` stages table (MUST update)
161	- `src/skf-test-skill/templates/test-report-template.md` anchor comments (MUST review)
162	- `src/skf-test-skill/references/report.md` (references `stepsCompleted` — should pick up `'hard-gate'` naturally)

4	
5	# Story 3.2: Per-Pipeline Quality Thresholds
6	
7	Status: done
8	
9	## Story
10	
11	As a pipeline operator,
12	I want each pipeline alias to declare its own default quality threshold,
13	So that deepwiki skills are held to 90% while forge skills default to 80%.
14	
15	## Acceptance Criteria
16	
17	1. **Given** a TS invocation within the deepwiki pipeline and no explicit override
18	   **When** TS looks up the threshold
19	   **Then** the default threshold is 90%
20	
21	2. **Given** a TS invocation within the forge pipeline and no explicit override
22	   **When** TS looks up the threshold
23	   **Then** the default threshold is 80%
24	
25	3. **Given** a TS invocation with the `TS[min:85]` override syntax
26	   **When** TS resolves the threshold
27	   **Then** the override value of 85% is used instead of the pipeline default
28	   **And** the source of the threshold (override vs default) is logged
29	
30	4. **Given** any pipeline and any threshold configuration
31	   **When** TS runs
32	   **Then** the hard gate (Story 3.1) is enforced before the soft gate regardless of threshold value
33	
34	## Tasks / Subtasks
35

55	## Dev Notes
56	
57	### Per-Pipeline Threshold Lookup Table
58	
59	The architecture specifies this exact table (architecture.md line 223):
60	
61	```
62	pipeline_threshold_defaults:
63	  deepwiki: 90
64	  forge: 80
65	  forge-quick: 80
66	  campaign: 90
67	```
68	
69	This table lives in `init.md` as a new §1b section. It is the canonical source of truth for pipeline-specific defaults. The alias expansion values in the forger (e.g., `TS[min:90]` in deepwiki) should match this table, but the table in TS is what TS itself references.
70	
71	### Threshold Resolution Precedence (Current → New)
72	
73	**Current** (score.md §1):
74	1. CLI `--threshold=N` → highest priority
75	2. `{defaultThreshold}` workflow-context variable (from customize.toml, default 80) → second
76	3. Fallback `80` → last resort
77	
78	**After this story** (score.md §1):
79	1. CLI `--threshold=N` → highest priority (user explicitly typed `--threshold`)
80	2. `{pipeline_default_threshold}` → pipeline-specific default from init.md §1b lookup table (only set when `{pipeline_alias}` is in workflow context)
81	3. `{defaultThreshold}` workflow-context variable (from customize.toml, default 80) → org-wide default
82	4. Fallback `80` → last resort
83	
84	### How `{pipeline_alias}` Reaches TS
85	
86	The forger orchestrates pipeline execution (src/skf-forger/SKILL.md Pipeline Mode). In step 4.c, it invokes each workflow with `{headless_mode}` = true and resolved arguments. The forger already knows the pipeline alias from step 1 (it parsed and expanded the alias).
87	
88	**Change needed:** the forger must also set `{pipeline_alias}` in the workflow data context for each workflow in the chain. This is a small addition to step 4.c — one new variable alongside `{headless_mode}`.
89	
90	For ad-hoc sequences (user typed `BS CS TS EX` directly, not via alias), `{pipeline_alias}` is `null` — no lookup table entry applies, and score.md falls through to `{defaultThreshold}`.
91	
92	### How `TS[min:N]` Interacts with Pipeline Default
93	
94	The bracket syntax `TS[min:N]` is parsed by the forger in step 1 and passed to TS as `--threshold=N`. Since CLI `--threshold` has highest precedence, it always wins over the pipeline default.
95	
96	This means:
97	- `deepwiki` → expands to `TS[min:90]` → forger passes `--threshold=90` → score.md uses 90 (CLI layer, but matches pipeline default)
98	- `deepwiki` with user override `TS[min:85]` → forger passes `--threshold=85` → score.md uses 85 (CLI override, pipeline default would have been 90)
99	- `forge` → expands to `TS` (no bracket) → no `--threshold` passed → score.md uses pipeline default 80 from lookup table
100	- Standalone `TS` invocation → no pipeline context → score.md uses `{defaultThreshold}` from customize.toml = 80
101	
102	### Threshold Source Logging
103	
104	Score.md §1 should record which layer supplied `effective_threshold`:
105	
106	| Scenario | Log message |
107	|----------|-------------|
108	| CLI/bracket override | `Threshold source: CLI override (85%)` |
109	| Pipeline default | `Threshold source: pipeline default (deepwiki → 90%)` |
110	| Customize.toml default | `Threshold source: workflow default (80%)` |
111	| Fallback | `Threshold source: bundled fallback (80%)` |
112	
113	This log line goes into the score report output and can optionally be added to the output frontmatter for traceability.
114	
115	### Step-Hard-Gate Chain Independence
116	
117	The step chain is (from story 3.1):
118	```
119	... → external-validators.md → step-hard-gate.md → score.md → report.md → ...
120	```
121	
122	The hard gate (`step-hard-gate.md`) scans for critical/high severity findings and blocks if any exist. It does NOT reference the threshold — it's purely severity-based. The threshold only matters in `score.md` (the soft gate). This means AC #4 is already satisfied by the existing chain — the hard gate runs before score.md regardless of which pipeline or threshold is in effect.
123	
124	No changes needed to `step-hard-gate.md`.
125	
126	### What Must NOT Change
127	
128	- `step-hard-gate.md` — no modifications; hard gate is threshold-independent
129	- `scoring-rules.md` — no modifications; scoring rules are pipeline-independent
130	- `coverage-check.md` / `coherence-check.md` — no modifications; analysis is threshold-independent
131	- `customize.toml` `default_threshold = 80` — this remains the org-wide default; pipeline defaults are a layer above it
132	- `compute-score.py` — no modifications; the script already accepts `threshold` as input JSON field
133	- Exit codes — no changes; same codes apply regardless of threshold source

5	# Story 3.3: Evidence Report on Threshold Fallback
6	
7	Status: done
8	
9	## Story
10	
11	As a skill author,
12	I want a written evidence report when the soft gate falls back from its target threshold to the 80% floor,
13	So that the quality compromise is documented and never silent.
14	
15	## Acceptance Criteria
16	
17	1. **Given** a skill that scores 82% against a 90% target and remediation has been attempted
18	   **When** TS falls back from the 90% target to the 80% floor
19	   **Then** an evidence report is written to `{forge_version}/evidence-report-fallback.md`
20	   **And** the report contains: attempted threshold, achieved score, specific findings preventing the higher threshold, remediation steps attempted, and the final accepted threshold
21	
22	2. **Given** a fallback occurs
23	   **When** the pipeline completes
24	   **Then** the fallback is visible in the pipeline output (not buried in the report alone)
25	   **And** the pipeline output includes the path to the evidence report
26	
27	3. **Given** a skill that meets its target threshold without fallback
28	   **When** TS completes scoring
29	   **Then** no evidence report is generated
30

34	  - [x] 1.1 Insert new §4b section between §4 (Determine Result) and §5 (Next Workflow Recommendation)
35	  - [x] 1.2 Implement fallback detection: if `result == "FAIL"` AND `totalScore >= 80` AND `effective_threshold > 80`, trigger fallback
36	  - [x] 1.3 On fallback trigger: override `result` to `"PASS"`, set `effective_threshold` to `80`, record `threshold_fallback: true`, `original_threshold`, `fallback_threshold: 80`, `evidence_report_path` in workflow context
37	  - [x] 1.4 Do NOT trigger fallback if `result == "INCONCLUSIVE"` — the evidence-floor verdict is never overridden by fallback
38	  - [x] 1.5 Do NOT trigger fallback if score < 80 — that's a genuine FAIL
39	  - [x] 1.6 Do NOT trigger fallback if `effective_threshold == 80` — there's nothing to fall back from

65	
66	## Dev Notes
67	
68	### Threshold Fallback Logic — Where It Lives
69	
70	The fallback is implemented entirely in `score.md` as a new §4b between the existing §4 (Determine Result) and §5 (Next Workflow Recommendation). The logic:
71	
72	```
73	IF result == "FAIL"
74	  AND totalScore >= 80
75	  AND effective_threshold > 80
76	  AND result != "INCONCLUSIVE"  (redundant but explicit — INCONCLUSIVE is never FAIL)
77	THEN
78	  → THRESHOLD FALLBACK triggered
79	  → Generate evidence report
80	  → Override result to "PASS"
81	  → Set effective_threshold = 80 for §5/§6/§7/§8
82	  → Record threshold_fallback = true in workflow context
83	```
84	
85	The fallback does NOT fire when:
86	- Score >= effective_threshold (already PASS — no fallback needed)
87	- Score < 80 (genuine FAIL — floor not met)
88	- effective_threshold == 80 (nothing to fall back from)
89	- Result is INCONCLUSIVE (evidence-floor verdict never overridden)
90	- Post-score caps (§3d) forced FAIL with totalScore >= 80 AND effective_threshold > 80 — the fallback DOES apply here because the cap penalized the score for tooling degradation, not quality; the evidence report should note the cap was active
91	
92	### Interaction with Post-Score Caps (§3d)
93	
94	If Cap 1 or Cap 2 fired (tooling degraded, docs-only without validators), the effective_score was capped to `threshold - 1`. If the original effective_threshold was, say, 90, then effective_score was capped to 89. Since 89 >= 80, the fallback fires. The evidence report MUST note the cap in the remediation section: "Score capped due to {cap reason} — address tooling to test at the higher threshold."
95	
96	### Evidence Report Template
97	
98	The evidence report at `{forge_version}/evidence-report-fallback.md` should contain:
99	
100	```markdown
101	# Evidence Report: Threshold Fallback
102	
103	**Skill:** {skill_name}
104	**Date:** {ISO-8601 timestamp}
105	**Run ID:** {run_id}
106	
107	## Threshold Summary
108	
109	| Field | Value |
110	|-------|-------|
111	| Attempted Threshold | {original_threshold}% |
112	| Achieved Score | {totalScore}% |
113	| Threshold Source | {threshold_source} |
114	| Final Accepted Threshold | 80% |
115	
116	## Findings Preventing Higher Threshold
117	
118	{For each gap entry from Coverage Analysis and Coherence Analysis:}
119	- **{GAP-NNN}: {title}** — Severity: {severity}, Category: {category}
120	
121	{Count: N critical, M high, P medium, Q low, R info findings}
122	
123	## Remediation Context
124	
125	{If prior test report exists:}
126	A prior test run was found at `{prior_report_path}`, indicating remediation was attempted between runs.
127	
128	{If no prior test report:}
129	No prior test report found for this skill version — this is the first test run.
130	
131	{If post-score cap was active:}
132	**Note:** Score was capped due to {cap_reason}. Address tooling limitations to test at the higher threshold.
133	
134	## Conclusion
135	
136	Skill accepted at 80% floor (original target: {original_threshold}%). The {N} findings above prevented meeting the higher threshold. Review and address findings before the next pipeline run to achieve the {original_threshold}% target.
137	```
138	
139	### How `{forge_version}` Resolves
140	
141	`{forge_version}` resolves to the versioned skill data path — typically `{forge_data_folder}/{skill_name}/{resolved_version}/`. This is the same directory where test reports (`test-report-{skill_name}-{run_id}.md`) are written. The evidence report sits alongside test reports for the same skill version, which is the correct semantic grouping.
142	
143	### Pipeline Output Visibility (AC #2)
144	
145	The fallback must be visible in THREE places:
146	1. **score.md §8 inline report** — the console output the user sees immediately after scoring
147	2. **report.md §6 final presentation** — the closing summary
148	3. **The evidence report file itself** — the persistent record
149	
150	The §8 report output adds a line like:
151	```
152	**Threshold fallback:** scored 82% against 90% target — accepted at 80% floor. Evidence report: {forge_version}/evidence-report-fallback.md
153	```
154	
155	### Result Contract Extension
156	
157	The on-disk JSON result contract (written in report.md §4c) gains three optional fields:
158	
159	```json
160	{
161	  "threshold_fallback": true,
162	  "original_threshold": 90,
163	  "evidence_report_path": "{forge_version}/evidence-report-fallback.md"
164	}
165	```
166	
167	These fields are absent when no fallback occurred (not `false`/`null` — absent). The headless stdout envelope adds `"threshold_fallback":true,"original_threshold":90` when fallback is active, omits them otherwise.
168	
169	### Step Chain — No New Step File
170	
171	This story does NOT create a new step file. The fallback logic lives inside score.md as a new §4b section. The step chain is unchanged:
172	
173	```
174	init.md → detect-mode.md → coverage-check.md → coherence-check.md → external-validators.md → step-hard-gate.md → score.md → report.md → health-check.md
175	```
176	
177	### What Must NOT Change
178	
179	- `step-hard-gate.md` — hard gate is severity-based, threshold-independent, unaffected by fallback
180	- `scoring-rules.md` — scoring rules don't change; gap severity definitions unaffected
181	- `coverage-check.md` / `coherence-check.md` — analysis steps unaffected; they still write findings in the same format
182	- `compute-score.py` — the scoring script still computes the same score; the fallback is a post-script evaluation decision
183	- `init.md` — threshold resolution in §1/§1b is unaffected; the fallback consumes the resolved threshold, doesn't change resolution
184	- `customize.toml` — `default_threshold = 80` remains the org-wide default; the 80% floor is a hardcoded constant in score.md, not configurable (the floor is architecturally fixed per FR-13)
185	- `external-validators.md` — validation step unaffected
186	- Exit codes — no new exit codes; a fallback PASS is still exit 0 (the skill passed, even if at a lower threshold)
187	
188	### Story 3.2 Learnings (Apply These)
189	
190	1. **Self-audit for internal references**: Do NOT include internal story IDs, epic numbers, or bmad keywords in the step file content or commit messages
191	2. **Cross-reference grep when modifying step files**: After modifying score.md and report.md, grep for any other files that reference `thresholdSource`, `testResult`, or the result contract schema to ensure consistency
192	3. **Section numbering convention**: Use §-prefixed sections within step files (§1, §2, etc.) — the new section in score.md should be §4b to slot between existing §4 and §5
193	4. **Backward compatibility**: Verify the fallback does NOT change behavior for standalone TS invocations at 80% threshold (no pipeline default → effective_threshold == 80 → no fallback possible → same behavior as before)
194	5. **Threshold source logging**: The fallback modifies the threshold but the `threshold_source` from §1 remains unchanged — it records where the *original* threshold came from; the evidence report records the fallback decision separately
195

198	After modifying score.md and report.md, verify these for consistency:
199	- `src/skf-test-skill/SKILL.md` — Outputs table should mention evidence report; Result Contract should include fallback fields
200	- `src/skf-test-skill/references/report.md` — §4c result contract JSON should include fallback fields; §6 presentation should surface fallback
201	- `src/shared/references/pipeline-contracts.md` — Circuit Breakers table for TS should note that scores between 80% and the pipeline default produce a fallback PASS (not a circuit breaker halt)
202	- `src/skf-test-skill/references/score.md` — §6/§7 should reference fallback state from §4b
203	
204	### Pipeline Circuit Breaker Interaction
205	
206	When TS runs in a pipeline with a per-pipeline threshold (e.g., deepwiki → 90%), and the score falls to 82%, TS internally falls back to 80% and reports PASS. The forger's circuit breaker checks the TS result: since TS reports PASS (with fallback notation), the pipeline **continues to EX** — it does not halt. The evidence report is the audit trail for why the quality compromise was accepted.
207	
208	If the score falls below 80%, TS reports FAIL with no fallback, and the circuit breaker halts the pipeline as normal.
209	
210	### Project Structure Notes
211	
212	- No new files created in `src/` — only modifications to existing step files
213	- `src/skf-test-skill/references/score.md` — gains §4b (threshold fallback + evidence report generation)
214	- `src/skf-test-skill/references/report.md` — §6 gains fallback notice, §4c gains fallback fields in result contract
215	- `src/skf-test-skill/SKILL.md` — Outputs and Result Contract documentation updated
216	- `test/test-skf-evidence-report-fallback.py` — new structural integration test file
217	- `package.json` — test:python command updated to include new test file
218	- Runtime artifact: `{forge_version}/evidence-report-fallback.md` — written at test time only when fallback occurs

4	
5	# Story 3.4: Auto-Shard at 400-Line Ceiling
6	
7	Status: done
8	
9	## Story
10	
11	As a skill author,
12	I want SKILL.md bodies exceeding 400 lines to be automatically sharded into references,
13	So that skills stay within the 500-line skill-check ceiling with comfortable headroom.
14	
15	## Acceptance Criteria
16	
17	1. **Given** a compiled SKILL.md with a body exceeding 400 lines
18	   **When** the auto-shard step runs post-compile
19	   **Then** navigational catalogs and deep-dive sections are moved to `references/`
20	   **And** the SKILL.md body is reduced to under 400 lines
21	   **And** hallucination guards (T1 markers, confidence annotations) remain inline in SKILL.md
22	
23	2. **Given** a compiled SKILL.md with a body of 350 lines
24	   **When** the auto-shard step runs post-compile
25	   **Then** no sharding is triggered and SKILL.md is unchanged
26	
27	3. **Given** a sharded SKILL.md
28	   **When** the resulting skill is validated by skill-check
29	   **Then** the skill passes the 500-line ceiling check
30	   **And** cross-references between SKILL.md and sharded reference files are intact
31	
32	## Tasks / Subtasks
33	
34	- [x] Task 1: Create step-auto-shard.md step file (AC: #1, #2, #3)
35	  - [x] 1.1 Create `src/skf-create-skill/references/step-auto-shard.md` with frontmatter `nextStepFile: 'validate.md'`
36	  - [x] 1.2 Implement §1 (Count Body Lines): count lines between frontmatter closing `---` and EOF (exclude trailing blank lines). Store as `body_line_count`. If ≤ 400 → skip to chain (proceed to validate.md)
37	  - [x] 1.3 Implement §2 (Selective Shard — Tier 2 Extraction): identify Tier 2 sections by `## Full` heading prefix (`## Full API Reference`, `## Full Type Definitions`, `## Full Integration Patterns`). Extract to `references/` starting with largest. Replace extracted section with a cross-reference line: `> See [Full API Reference](references/full-api-reference.md)` (kebab-case filename)
38	  - [x] 1.4 Implement §3 (Tier 1 Preservation Check): verify ALL Tier 1 sections remain inline after extraction — Overview, Quick Start, Common Workflows, Key API Summary, Migration & Deprecation Warnings (if present), Key Types, Architecture at a Glance, CLI (if present), Scripts & Assets (if present), Manual Sections. HALT if any Tier 1 section was moved
39	  - [x] 1.5 Implement §4 (Post-Shard Validation): re-count body lines; if still > 400 after extracting all Tier 2 sections, reduce oversized Tier 1 content (Key API Summary, Architecture at a Glance) — do NOT extract Tier 1 to references
40	  - [x] 1.6 Implement §5 (Cross-Reference Integrity): verify each extracted reference file exists in `references/` directory and each cross-reference link in SKILL.md resolves correctly
41	  - [x] 1.7 Log auto-shard results to context: `auto_shard_triggered: true/false`, `sections_extracted: [names]`, `body_lines_before`, `body_lines_after`
42	
43	- [x] Task 2: Update step chain — step-doc-sources.md (AC: #1, #2)
44	  - [x] 2.1 Change `nextStepFile` in `src/skf-create-skill/references/step-doc-sources.md` frontmatter from `'validate.md'` to `'step-auto-shard.md'`
45	
46	- [x] Task 3: Update CS SKILL.md Stages table (AC: #1, #2)
47	  - [x] 3.1 Add row after step 5a: `| 5b | Auto-Shard | references/step-auto-shard.md | Yes |`
48	
49	- [x] Task 4: Create structural integration tests (AC: #1, #2, #3)
50	  - [x] 4.1 Create `test/test-skf-auto-shard.py` following the pattern from `test/test-skf-evidence-report-fallback.py`
51	  - [x] 4.2 Register the new test file in `package.json` test:python command
52	  - [x] 4.3 Test cases: step-auto-shard.md exists, nextStepFile is validate.md, 400-line threshold constant present, Tier 1 section names listed for preservation, Tier 2 `## Full` heading pattern present, cross-reference link format present, step-doc-sources.md chains to step-auto-shard.md, SKILL.md Stages table includes Auto-Shard row, step-auto-shard.md has §-prefixed sections, context logging variables present (auto_shard_triggered, sections_extracted, body_lines_before, body_lines_after)
53	
54	## Dev Notes
55	
56	### Where Auto-Shard Fits in the Step Chain
57	
58	The auto-shard step slots between doc-sources (5a) and validate (6). The updated chain:
59	
60	```
61	compile.md → step-doc-sources.md → step-auto-shard.md → validate.md → generate-artifacts.md → report.md → health-check.md
62	```
63	
64	The auto-shard at 400 lines is a **proactive** size reduction. validate.md §4 (which triggers on skill-check's 500-line `body.max_lines` failure) remains as a **safety net**. In most cases, auto-shard handles the extraction and validate §4 becomes a no-op.
65	
66	### Relationship to validate.md §4 (split-body)
67	
68	validate.md §4 already handles oversized bodies at the 500-line ceiling with selective Tier 2 extraction. The auto-shard step at 400 lines:
69	- Uses the **same Tier 2 selective extraction approach** (extract `## Full` sections starting with largest)
70	- Follows the **same Tier 1 preservation invariant** (Tier 1 sections must never move to references/)
71	- Runs **earlier in the chain** to provide headroom below the 500-line hard limit
72	- Does NOT use `npx skill-check split-body --write` — it is a step-file-level extraction, not a tool invocation
73	- Does NOT invoke the Description Guard Protocol — it does not modify frontmatter, only body content
74	
75	If auto-shard already reduced the body to under 400 lines, validate.md §4 will see no `body.max_lines` failure from skill-check and skip its split logic entirely.
76	
77	### Tier 1 vs Tier 2 Section Names
78	
79	**Tier 1 — Always Inline (never extract):**
80	- Overview, Quick Start, Common Workflows, Key API Summary, Migration & Deprecation Warnings (conditional), Key Types, Architecture at a Glance, CLI (conditional), Scripts & Assets (conditional), Manual Sections
81	
82	**Tier 2 — Reference-Eligible (extract targets):**
83	- `## Full API Reference` → `references/full-api-reference.md`
84	- `## Full Type Definitions` → `references/full-type-definitions.md`
85	- `## Full Integration Patterns` → `references/full-integration-patterns.md`
86	
87	Identification: Tier 2 sections have the `## Full` heading prefix. This is the same heuristic used by validate.md §4.
88	
89	### Cross-Reference Format
90	
91	When a Tier 2 section is extracted, replace it in SKILL.md with a blockquote cross-reference:
92	
93	```markdown
94	> See [Full API Reference](references/full-api-reference.md)
95	```
96	
97	This matches the existing pattern used by validate.md §4's selective extraction. The reference file contains the complete section content with its `##` heading preserved.
98	
99	### Body Line Counting
100	
101	"Body" = all lines after the frontmatter closing `---` to EOF (excluding trailing blank lines). This matches the metric that `skill-check` uses for `body.max_lines`. The 400-line threshold provides 100 lines of headroom below the 500-line ceiling.
102	
103	### What Must NOT Change
104	
105	- `validate.md` — the §4 split-body logic stays as-is (safety net at 500 lines)
106	- `compile.md` — compile step is unchanged; auto-shard runs after compile, not during
107	- `generate-artifacts.md` — writes whatever SKILL.md + references/ exist post-validate; no changes needed (it already handles `ref_count >= 0`)
108	- The Description Guard Protocol — auto-shard does not touch frontmatter, only body sections
109	- `compile-assembly-rules.md` — assembly rules unchanged; auto-shard is post-assembly
110	
111	### Step File Conventions (from Stories 3.1–3.3)
112	
113	1. Frontmatter with `nextStepFile` for chaining
114	2. `# Step 5b: Auto-Shard` heading matching the Stages table
115	3. `## STEP GOAL:` heading
116	4. `## Rules` section
117	5. `## MANDATORY SEQUENCE` with §-prefixed subsections
118	6. Auto-proceed step — no user interaction gates
119	7. Graceful failure — if body is under threshold, skip cleanly
120	8. Self-audit: NO internal story IDs, epic numbers, or bmad keywords in step file content
121	
122	### Auto-Shard Algorithm
123	
124	```
125	§1 Count body lines
126	  IF body_lines <= 400 → log "auto-shard: skipped (body {N} lines)", chain to validate.md
127	  ELSE → proceed to §2
128	
129	§2 Selective Tier 2 extraction
130	  FOR EACH Tier 2 section (sorted by line count DESC):
131	    - Extract section content to references/{kebab-case-name}.md
132	    - Replace section in SKILL.md with cross-reference blockquote
133	    - Re-count body lines
134	    - IF body_lines <= 400 → stop extracting, proceed to §3
135	
136	§3 Tier 1 preservation check
137	  FOR EACH known Tier 1 heading:
138	    - Verify heading exists in SKILL.md body
139	    - IF missing → HALT: "Auto-shard removed Tier 1 section {name}. Aborting."
140	
141	§4 Post-shard validation
142	  Re-count body lines
143	  IF still > 400 after all Tier 2 extracted:
144	    - Trim oversized Tier 1 sections (Key API Summary, Architecture at a Glance)
145	    - Do NOT move Tier 1 to references
146	
147	§5 Cross-reference integrity
148	  FOR EACH extracted reference file:
149	    - Verify file exists at expected path
150	    - Verify cross-reference link in SKILL.md points to it
151	  Log: "auto-shard: {N} sections extracted, body reduced from {before} to {after} lines"
152	```
153	
154	### Story 3.3 Learnings (Apply These)
155	
156	1. **Self-audit for internal references**: Do NOT include internal story IDs, epic numbers, or bmad keywords in the step file content or commit messages
157	2. **Cross-reference grep when modifying step files**: After modifying step-doc-sources.md and creating step-auto-shard.md, grep for any other files that reference the step chain order or `step-doc-sources.md` → `validate.md` chaining
158	3. **Section numbering convention**: Use §-prefixed sections (§1, §2, etc.) within the step file
159	4. **Register test file in package.json**: The `test:python` command enumerates every test path — adding `test/test-skf-auto-shard.py` without updating package.json leaves it dormant in CI
160	
161	### Story 3.1 Learnings (Apply These)
162	
163	1. **Report.md stepsCompleted chain**: Story 3.1 discovered that report.md in TS hardcodes a canonical step chain. Check whether CS's report.md has a similar pattern — if it does, ensure 'auto-shard' is added
164	2. **Stages table alignment**: Step numbering in the Stages table must match the `# Step N:` heading in the step file
165

170	- `src/skf-create-skill/references/step-doc-sources.md` — nextStepFile must be step-auto-shard.md
171	- `src/skf-create-skill/references/validate.md` — §4 remains unchanged (safety net)
172	- `src/skf-create-skill/references/report.md` — check for hardcoded step chain/stepsCompleted
173	- `src/skf-create-skill/references/generate-artifacts.md` — `ref_count` handling already supports 0 or N references; no changes needed
174	- `src/skf-create-skill/assets/compile-assembly-rules.md` — mentions split-body in assembly rules; no changes needed (auto-shard is a separate pre-validate step, not a compile-time concern)
175

179	- Modified: `src/skf-create-skill/references/step-doc-sources.md` — nextStepFile chain update
180	- Modified: `src/skf-create-skill/SKILL.md` — Stages table row addition

4	
5	# Story 3.5: Pre-Apply Workaround Registry
6	
7	Status: done
8	
9	## Story
10	
11	As a pipeline operator,
12	I want known workarounds to be automatically applied before each pipeline iteration,
13	So that previously-solved issues are not re-discovered and manually fixed each time.
14	
15	## Acceptance Criteria
16	
17	1. **Given** a `_known-workarounds.yaml` registry with a fingerprint matching content in the target directory
18	   **When** `skf-preapply.py --target-dir <path>` runs
19	   **Then** the matching fix is applied to the target content
20	   **And** the applied fix is logged to `forge-data/{skill-name}/{version}/preapply-log.json`
21	
22	2. **Given** a registry with no fingerprints matching the target content
23	   **When** `skf-preapply.py` runs
24	   **Then** no fixes are applied and the log records zero matches
25	
26	3. **Given** both a shared seed registry and a project-local registry with a colliding fingerprint
27	   **When** `skf-preapply.py --target-dir <path> --local-registry <path>` runs
28	   **Then** the project-local fix wins on collision
29	   **And** the shared seed is loaded first, project-local merged on top
30	
31	4. **Given** a new workaround discovered during a pipeline run
32	   **When** it is recorded
33	   **Then** it is appended to the project-local registry only, never to the shared seed
34	
35	5. **Given** a fingerprint entry with `regex: true`
36	   **When** matching is performed
37	   **Then** regex matching is used instead of the default substring matching
38	
39	## Tasks / Subtasks
40	
41	- [x] Task 1: Create the `_known-workarounds.yaml` shared seed registry (AC: #1, #3, #4)
42	  - [x] 1.1 Create `src/shared/_known-workarounds.yaml` with `version: 1` header and `workarounds:` array
43	  - [x] 1.2 Populate with 25 GMC campaign fingerprint-to-fix pairs following the schema: `{fingerprint, fix, source, severity, description}` per entry
44	  - [x] 1.3 Include entries spanning severity levels (low, medium, high) with a mix of substring and regex fingerprints
45	  - [x] 1.4 Add `regex: true` flag on entries that require regex matching (default is substring)
46	
47	- [x] Task 2: Create `skf-preapply.py` shared script (AC: #1, #2, #3, #5)
48	  - [x] 2.1 Create `src/shared/scripts/skf-preapply.py` with PEP 723 header (`requires-python = ">=3.9"`, `dependencies = ["pyyaml"]`)

77	
78	## Dev Notes
79	
80	### Script Interface (from Architecture)
81	
82	```
83	skf-preapply.py
84	Args: --target-dir <path> [--registry <path>] [--local-registry <path>]
85	Stdout: JSON {applied[], skipped_count, registry_version}
86	Exit: 0 (success), 2 (error)
87	```
88	
89	Note: exit code is `0` for both "applied fixes" and "zero matches" — both are successful runs. Exit `1` is NOT used by this script (unlike detect-docs where `1` = "none found"). The architecture specifies only exit `0` and `2` for preapply.
90	
91	### Registry Schema (from Architecture)
92	
93	```yaml
94	version: 1
95	workarounds:
96	  - fingerprint: string     # pattern to match in target content
97	    fix: string             # correction text or instruction
98	    source: string          # where the workaround was discovered
99	    severity: low|medium|high
100	    description: string     # one-line human summary
101	    regex: true             # optional — use regex matching instead of substring
102	```
103	
104	### Merge Strategy
105	
106	1. Load shared seed registry (`src/shared/_known-workarounds.yaml` or `--registry` path)
107	2. Load project-local registry (`--local-registry` path, if provided)
108	3. Merge: iterate project-local entries; on fingerprint collision (same `fingerprint` string), local entry replaces shared entry
109	4. Merged registry is ephemeral — never persisted as a combined file
110	
111	**Anti-pattern (from Architecture):** Modifying the shared seed file during a pipeline run. The shared seed is an SKF source file — only maintainer PRs change it.
112	
113	### Fingerprint Matching
114	
115	- Default: substring match — `fingerprint` string is searched within each target file's content
116	- Regex opt-in: when entry has `regex: true`, `fingerprint` is compiled as a Python `re` pattern
117	- Match target: all `.md` files within `--target-dir` (step files, SKILL.md, reference docs)
118	- Files are read with `encoding="utf-8"` (cross-platform safety per PR #311/#316/#366/#368/#387)
119	
120	### Fix Application
121	
122	When a fingerprint matches:
123	1. Apply the `fix` as a replacement for the matched content
124	2. Log the application to the `applied` array in stdout JSON
125	3. If `--log-dir` is provided, write detailed log to `preapply-log.json` at that path
126	
127	### Consumers of Pre-Apply
128	
129	- **All pipeline runs:** Pre-apply runs before each pipeline iteration (shared module)
130	- **Campaign step-05 (skill-loop):** Per-skill pre-apply → kickoff → AN → BS → CS → TS
131	- **Standalone invocation:** `uv run python src/shared/scripts/skf-preapply.py --target-dir <path>`
132	
133	### Shared Script Conventions (from Architecture + Stories 3.1–3.4)
134	
135	1. **PEP 723 header:** `requires-python = ">=3.9"`, `dependencies = ["pyyaml"]`
136	2. **Module docstring:** describe CLI, input/output, exit codes
137	3. **argparse:** GNU-style long flags, kebab-case (`--target-dir`, not `--target_dir`)
138	4. **JSON stdout:** one object, no pretty-print in non-interactive mode
139	5. **Errors:** JSON `{"error": "message", "code": "ERROR_TYPE"}` to stderr on exit 2
140	6. **No global state, no env var dependencies** beyond what uv provides
141	7. **Invocation:** `uv run python src/shared/scripts/skf-preapply.py --target-dir <path>`
142	8. **Paths:** Use `Path.as_posix()` in assertions, not `str()` — cross-platform safety
143	9. **File reads:** Always `encoding="utf-8"` — avoid cp1252 mojibake on Windows
144	10. **File writes:** Use `os.open` with `O_BINARY` flag for byte-exact writes if needed
145	
146	### What the GMC Seed Fingerprints Cover
147	
148	The 25 GMC campaign fingerprints capture recurring workarounds discovered during batch skill-forging campaigns. They address common issues like:
149	- Hallucinated API endpoints or function signatures in compiled skills
150	- Stale version references carried from outdated documentation
151	- Inconsistent heading structures that fail skill-check validation
152	- Missing provenance markers (T1 annotations, confidence tiers)
153	- Repeated citation format errors
154	- Common markdown lint violations in generated content
155	- Section ordering violations against the skill-sections spec
156	- Broken cross-reference links to non-existent reference files
157	
158	Each fingerprint is a stable textual pattern that recurs across multiple skill compilations. The fix is the deterministic correction.
159	
160	### Project Structure Notes
161	
162	New files:
163	- `src/shared/scripts/skf-preapply.py` — the pre-apply shared script
164	- `src/shared/_known-workarounds.yaml` — shared seed registry (25 GMC fingerprints)
165	- `test/test-skf-preapply.py` — structural integration tests
166	
167	Modified files:
168	- `package.json` — add `test/test-skf-preapply.py` to `test:python` command
169	
170	No existing step files or workflows are modified — this story creates the standalone shared module. Integration into pipeline flows (calling pre-apply before each iteration) will happen at the pipeline level in downstream stories or campaign (Story 4.6).
171	
172	### Story 3.4 Learnings (Apply These)
173	
174	1. **Register test file in package.json:** The `test:python` command enumerates every test path — adding `test/test-skf-preapply.py` without updating it leaves the test dormant in CI
175	2. **Self-audit for internal references:** Do NOT include internal story IDs, epic numbers, or bmad keywords in the script content, registry comments, or commit messages
176	3. **Cross-platform path assertions:** Use `Path.as_posix()` not `str()` in test assertions; read with `encoding="utf-8"`; windows-latest CI is the gatekeeper
177	4. **Structural test pattern:** Follow `test-skf-evidence-report-fallback.py` for structural tests (pathlib-based, file existence, content regex matching) and `test-skf-detect-docs.py` for functional tests (importlib dynamic loading, subprocess CLI verification)
178	
179	### Story 3.1 Learnings (Apply These)
180	
181	1. **Report.md stepsCompleted chain:** Not applicable to this story — pre-apply is a shared script, not a step in a workflow chain
182	2. **Stages table alignment:** Not applicable — no new step file in any workflow's Stages table
183	
184	### What Must NOT Change
185	
186	- Existing shared scripts — pre-apply is a new script, no modifications to existing ones
187	- The shared seed registry (`src/shared/_known-workarounds.yaml`) must never be modified at runtime
188	- `forge-data/` structure — pre-apply writes logs there but does not modify the directory layout
189
