5	# Story 3.6: Doc-Rot Correction Hooks
6	
7	Status: done
8	
9	## Story
10	
11	As a skill author,
12	I want corrected items in feeder artifacts to be automatically annotated post-compile,
13	So that doc-rot corrections are surfaced deterministically without relying on AI judgment.
14	
15	## Acceptance Criteria
16	
17	1. **Given** a feeder artifact containing corrected content (identifiable via grep patterns)
18	   **When** the doc-rot hook fires post-compile
19	   **Then** correction blocks (`## CORRECTION`) are annotated in the compiled output
20	
21	2. **Given** the doc-rot hook
22	   **When** it performs matching
23	   **Then** matching is grep-based and deterministic (no AI judgment involved)
24	
25	3. **Given** a standalone deepwiki pipeline run (not a campaign)
26	   **When** CS completes compilation
27	   **Then** the doc-rot hook fires (it is not campaign-only)
28	
29	4. **Given** a feeder artifact with no corrected content
30	   **When** the doc-rot hook fires
31	   **Then** no correction blocks are added and the output is unchanged
32	
33	## Tasks / Subtasks
34	
35	- [x] Task 1: Create `step-doc-rot.md` step file (AC: #1, #2, #3, #4)
36	  - [x] 1.1 Create `src/skf-create-skill/references/step-doc-rot.md` with frontmatter `nextStepFile: 'validate.md'`
37	  - [x] 1.2 Define the step goal: scan feeder artifacts for doc-rot correction indicators and annotate compiled output with `## CORRECTION` blocks
38	  - [x] 1.3 Define the Rules section: auto-proceed, no user interaction, graceful skip when no corrections found, do not modify anything except the compiled SKILL.md and references
39	  - [x] 1.4 Define the mandatory grep pattern list for correction detection (see Dev Notes — Grep Patterns)
40	  - [x] 1.5 Define the feeder artifact scan targets: evidence-report.md, provenance-map.json (T2/T3 entries with temporal annotations), temporal docs in context from step 3b/4
41	  - [x] 1.6 Define the `## CORRECTION` block format: heading, source citation, pattern matched, affected API/section
42	  - [x] 1.7 Define the insertion logic: correction blocks appended after the relevant section in SKILL.md (or at end if section cannot be determined)
43	  - [x] 1.8 Define the graceful skip path: no feeder artifacts with corrections → log skip message → proceed to validate
44	  - [x] 1.9 Define the auto-proceed to `{nextStepFile}` at end of step

62	
63	## Dev Notes
64	
65	### Where This Step Fits in the CS Pipeline
66	
67	The current CS step chain (post-compile):
68	```
69	5. Compile → 5a. Doc Sources → 5b. Auto-Shard → 6. Validate → 7. Generate Artifacts → 8. Report → 9. Health Check
70	```
71	
72	After this story:
73	```
74	5. Compile → 5a. Doc Sources → 5b. Auto-Shard → 5c. Doc-Rot → 6. Validate → 7. Generate Artifacts → 8. Report → 9. Health Check
75	```
76	
77	The chain update requires TWO changes:
78	1. `step-auto-shard.md` frontmatter: `nextStepFile: 'step-doc-rot.md'` (currently `'validate.md'`)
79	2. `step-doc-rot.md` frontmatter: `nextStepFile: 'validate.md'`
80	
81	### Feeder Artifact Scan Targets
82	
83	"Feeder artifacts" in the CS workflow are the intermediate artifacts produced during extraction and enrichment that fed the compilation:
84	
85	1. **Evidence report** (`forge-data/{skill-name}/{version}/evidence-report.md`) — contains compile warnings, signature conflicts, and enrichment notes that may reference corrections
86	2. **Provenance map** (`forge-data/{skill-name}/{version}/provenance-map.json`) — T2/T3 entries with temporal annotations (deprecation markers, breaking change citations from QMD enrichment)
87	3. **Temporal context in workflow context** — changelogs, migration guides, and issue/PR data fetched by step 3b and enriched by step 4. If a temporal source noted a breaking change or deprecation for a documented API, that's a correction signal
88	4. **The compiled SKILL.md itself** — may contain T2 `[QMD:...]` annotations or T3 `[DOC:...]` citations that reference corrections in their source material
89	
90	### Grep Patterns for Correction Detection
91	
92	The step file MUST define an explicit, enumerated list of grep patterns. The LLM executing the step uses these patterns mechanically — no open-ended "assess whether content seems corrected." Minimum required patterns:
93	
94	| Pattern | Category | Target |
95	|---------|----------|--------|
96	| `deprecated` / `@deprecated` | Deprecation | API entries marked deprecated in temporal docs or source |
97	| `breaking change` / `BREAKING` | Breaking change | Changelog/migration guide entries |
98	| `removed in` / `was removed` | Removal | Functions/params removed between versions |
99	| `renamed to` / `renamed from` | Rename | API renames noted in changelogs |
100	| `superseded by` / `replaced by` | Supersession | APIs replaced by newer alternatives |
101	| `no longer supported` | End of life | Features/APIs dropped from support |
102	| `migration required` | Migration | Mandatory migration steps for consumers |
103	| `signature changed` | Signature change | Provenance map entries with T1/T2 signature conflicts |
104	
105	These patterns are scanned as case-insensitive substring matches against feeder artifact content. The step file defines them as a literal list, not as configurable input.
106	
107	### `## CORRECTION` Block Format
108	
109	When a correction is detected, the step annotates the compiled SKILL.md with a correction block:
110	
111	```markdown
112	## CORRECTION
113	
114	**Source:** {feeder_artifact_path_or_citation}
115	**Pattern:** {matched_pattern}
116	**Affected:** {function_name_or_section}
117	**Detail:** {one-line excerpt from the feeder artifact showing the correction context}
118	```
119	
120	Correction blocks are inserted:
121	- **After the relevant API section** in SKILL.md if the affected function/section can be identified
122	- **At the end of SKILL.md body** (before any trailing sections) if the affected section cannot be determined
123	- **Never inside frontmatter** — body content only
124	
125	Multiple corrections produce multiple `## CORRECTION` blocks. Each block is self-contained with its source citation.
126	
127	### Architecture Compliance
128	
129	From `architecture.md`:
130	- **FR-19:** "Post-compile hook in CS greps feeder artifacts for corrected items, annotates with correction blocks"
131	- **Step file pattern:** frontmatter with `nextStepFile`, RULES section, MANDATORY SEQUENCE, auto-proceed at end
132	- **Anti-pattern (from Architecture):** "A step that stores intermediate results in conversation context instead of writing to disk." — If corrections are found, write them to the staged SKILL.md on disk
133	- **Scope:** This step runs in ALL CS invocations (standalone forge, deepwiki, campaign per-skill loop) — it is NOT campaign-only
134	
135	### Campaign Integration (Future — NOT This Story)
136	
137	Campaign step-05 (skill-loop) references doc-rot as part of the per-skill sequence: `pre-apply → kickoff → AN → BS → CS → TS → mark complete → propagate findings → doc-rot check`. This story makes doc-rot fire INSIDE CS (as step 5c), so campaign gets it automatically when it invokes CS. No separate campaign integration is needed.

1	# Epic 2 Retrospective — deepwiki: Zero-Ceremony Skill Creation
2	
3	**Date:** 2026-05-26
4	**Facilitator:** bmad-retrospective (Amelia)
5	**Project Lead:** Armel
6	**Epic status at retro:** all 9 stories `done`; epic `in-progress` (to be flipped `done`); retrospective key `epic-2-retrospective` flipped `optional` → `done`.
7	**Previous retrospective:** epic-1-retro-2026-05-26.md (Source Intelligence Foundation — same day, earlier session).
8	
9	## Summary
10	
11	Epic 2 delivered the complete deepwiki zero-ceremony skill creation pipeline: 9 stories implementing auto-scope (AN), auto-brief with validation (BS), auto-decomposition, docs-only path, coexistence detection, version pinning, the `deepwiki` pipeline alias, `onboard` deprecation, and v1.9 user documentation. All 9 stories shipped on **2026-05-26/27** on the `deepwiki-zero-ceremony-skill-creation` branch.
12	
13	| Story | Outcome |
14	|-------|---------|
15	| 2.1 Auto-scope mode for AN | `step-auto-scope.md` + `step-shape-detect.md` created. `[auto]` routing in init.md. Review fixed 1 HIGH (go.mod references unsupported by shape-detect), 1 MEDIUM. |
16	| 2.2 Auto-brief generation for BS | `step-auto-brief.md` created. Doc detection enrichment with graceful degradation. Review fixed envelope emitter to support `mode` field (schema + script + tests). |
17	| 2.3 Auto-brief validation with progressive fallback | `step-auto-validate.md` created. Approve/Edit/Reject gate with headless auto-approve. Review fixed 4 issues: restored error envelope (critical), stale cross-ref, [E]dit path ordering, [R]eject field preservation. |
18	| 2.4 Auto-decomposition for massive repos | §3a/§4a/§5a/§6a conditional branch in step-auto-scope.md. Two thresholds (500 exports, 3 packages) with `[PENDING VALIDATION]` markers. Review fixed 3 issues (stale mapping note, missing validation markers, absent merge threshold). |
19	| 2.5 Docs-only deepwiki path | §0/§0a URL type detection and docs-only short-circuit. Conditional skip in step-auto-brief.md. Review fixed exit code alignment (2→3) and halt_reason alignment. |
20	| 2.6 Coexistence detection | §0c coexistence gate with A/M/S actions. Inventory-based matching. `-wiki` suffix for alongside. Review fixed 3 MEDIUM (envelope field, multi-match disambiguation, URL normalization). |
21	| 2.7 Version pinning for deepwiki | `skf-validate-pins.py` created (new shared script, 32 tests). §0b pin resolution gate. Review fixed 3 MEDIUM (redundant API call, no-op CLI test, internal story ID references). |
22	| 2.8 deepwiki pipeline alias + onboard deprecation | `deepwiki` alias registered in forger + pipeline-contracts.md. `[auto]` bracket modifier documented. `onboard` deprecation notice. Review fixed 2 issues (missing AN→BS data flow entry, internal story ref). |
23	| 2.9 v1.9 documentation | `docs/deepwiki.md` created. `docs/workflows.md` and `docs/bmad-synergy.md` updated. Review added deepwiki sidebar entry to astro config. |
24	
25	Total new test files: 1 (`test-skf-validate-pins.py` with 32 tests). Total test count: 1766 (from 1765).
26	
27	## What went well
28	
29	1. **Progressive story layering.** Stories built cleanly on each other: 2.1 (auto-scope) → 2.2 (auto-brief) → 2.3 (validation gate) → 2.4 (decomposition) → 2.5 (docs-only) → 2.6 (coexistence) → 2.7 (pinning) → 2.8 (alias registration) → 2.9 (documentation). Each story added a conditional branch or gate to step-auto-scope.md without disrupting previous work. The section numbering scheme (§0, §0a, §0b, §0c, §1, ...) accommodated insertions elegantly.
30	
31	2. **Consistent `[auto]` routing pattern across AN and BS.** Story 2.1 established the pattern (§2b auto check in init.md, route to step-auto-scope.md), and Story 2.2 replicated it identically (§1b auto check in gather-intent.md, route to step-auto-brief.md). Both used pure conditional branches with zero changes to interactive paths. This pattern is now a proven, repeatable convention for future auto-mode extensions.
32	
33	3. **Envelope extension discipline.** No new envelope types were created across all 9 stories. Every new field (`mode`, `source_type`, `pinned_ref`, `pinned_version`, `coexistence`) was added to existing `SKF_ANALYZE_RESULT_JSON` and `SKF_BRIEF_RESULT_JSON` envelopes. Backward compatibility was maintained throughout — existing consumers ignore unknown fields.
34	
35	4. **Story intelligence carried forward effectively.** Every story's dev notes explicitly referenced patterns and learnings from prior stories (and the Epic 1 retro). The "Previous Story Intelligence" section in each story file became a genuine knowledge transfer mechanism. Story 2.7's review caught a no-op CLI test — the same class of bug the Epic 1 retro flagged in Story 1.2. The retro warning was heeded and the pattern was detected.
36	
37	5. **Documentation as a first-class deliverable.** Story 2.9 was a dedicated documentation story, not an afterthought. It produced `docs/deepwiki.md` as a focused user guide, updated `docs/workflows.md` with alias table and examples, and added a standalone-user section to `docs/bmad-synergy.md`. The review also caught the missing sidebar entry in `astro.config.mjs`.
38	
39	6. **Graceful degradation consistently applied.** Every auto feature has a defined fallback: unknown shape → interactive AN, doc detection failure → brief without docs, brief rejection → interactive BS, URL unreachable → clear error, no releases → HEAD, no existing skills → proceed silently. This pattern was established in the architecture and consistently implemented.
40	
41	7. **Code review discipline from Epic 1 sustained.** Every story went through structured review. Total across the epic: ~19 findings patched (2 CRITICAL, 8 MEDIUM, 5 LOW, 4 informational). No unpatched CRITICAL or HIGH findings at close.
42	
43	## Patterns across all nine stories
44	
45	### Review consistently finds real issues
46	
47	| Story | Patches | Types of Findings |
48	|-------|---------|-------------------|
49	| 2.1 | 2 | Unsupported manifest type (HIGH), excess references |
50	| 2.2 | 2 | Envelope schema/script update, doc detection clarification |
51	| 2.3 | 4 | Dangling refs (CRITICAL), stale cross-ref, path ordering, field preservation |
52	| 2.4 | 3 | Stale mapping note, missing validation markers, absent threshold |
53	| 2.5 | 2 | Exit code misalignment, halt_reason misalignment |
54	| 2.6 | 3 | Missing envelope field, multi-match gap, URL normalization |
55	| 2.7 | 3 | Redundant API call, no-op test, internal story ref |
56	| 2.8 | 2 | Missing data flow entry, internal story ref |
57	| 2.9 | 1 | Missing sidebar entry |
58	
59	The "internal story reference in shipped source" finding appeared in both 2.7 and 2.8 — indicating a systematic habit that reviews consistently caught. The "no-op test" finding in 2.7 (same class as Epic 1's Story 1.2) confirms that mock-based testing remains a risk area.
60	
61	### Step-file section numbering accommodates progressive insertion
62	
63	step-auto-scope.md grew from 11 sections (§1-§11) in Story 2.1 to a flow with pre-sections (§0, §0a, §0b, §0c) and branching sections (§3a, §4a, §5a, §6a). The numbering convention (§0 before §1, §3a as a branch from §3) kept the document readable and allowed each story to insert without renumbering. This should be documented as a recommended pattern for future step file evolution.
64	
65	### Backward compatibility verified systematically in every story
66	
67	Every story explicitly checked that interactive paths, existing pipeline aliases (forge, forge-quick, onboard, maintain), and headless-without-auto mode were unaffected. The `[auto]` flag is the single entry point for all new behavior — nothing fires without it. Zero regressions across the full test suite.
68	
69	### One new shared script (vs. 9 new step files)
70	
71	Epic 2 created only one new Python script (`skf-validate-pins.py`), versus 4 new step files and modifications to 2 existing step files. This reflects the architectural principle that heuristic analysis (shape-to-scope mapping, decomposition boundaries, URL type detection) belongs in step files (LLM judgment), while deterministic computation (tag validation, semver parsing) goes in Python scripts.
72	
73	## Challenges and growth areas
74	
75	1. **Internal reference leakage into shipped source.** Stories 2.7 and 2.8 both shipped with internal story ID references ("Story 2.7", "story 2.7") in source files. Both were caught in review, but the pattern indicates a systematic tendency. The dev agent should be trained to self-audit for internal references before review.
76	
77	2. **Envelope schema evolution complexity.** While no new envelope types were created (good), the growing number of optional fields on existing envelopes (`mode`, `source_type`, `pinned_ref`, `pinned_version`, `coexistence`, `redirect_to`, `skipped_reason`) makes the envelope contract harder to reason about. No formal schema validation exists for these envelopes at runtime — correctness relies on step file discipline.
78	
79	3. **step-auto-scope.md accumulated significant complexity.** By Story 2.7, step-auto-scope.md contains: §0 (URL detection), §0b (pin resolution), §0c (coexistence detection), §0a (docs-only short-circuit), §1-§3 (standard flow), §3a (decomposition threshold), §4-§6 (single scope), §4a-§6a (multi-scope), §7-§11 (report/brief/envelope). This is the most complex step file in SKF. While each addition was clean in isolation, the aggregate complexity warrants monitoring.
80	
81	4. **No empirical validation of the complete deepwiki flow.** All stories were validated with `npm test` (structural tests), but the end-to-end deepwiki pipeline (`deepwiki <repo-url>`) has not been run against a real repository. The auto-scope heuristics, decomposition thresholds, and pin resolution all carry `[PENDING VALIDATION]` markers. The first real-world run will be the real test.
82	
83	5. **Story 2.3 review found a CRITICAL issue (dangling §7 references).** When step-auto-brief.md was modified to remove envelope emission, 4 downstream references to §7 became dangling. This was caught in review, but it highlights the risk of modifying step files that other step files cross-reference. Step file refactoring needs to include a cross-reference grep.
84

86	
87	| Item | Source | Severity | Impact on Epic 3 |
88	|------|--------|----------|-------------------|
89	| `[PENDING VALIDATION]` on 500-export threshold | Story 2.4 | Medium | None (decomposition is deepwiki-only; Epic 3 doesn't touch it) |
90	| `[PENDING VALIDATION]` on 3-package threshold | Story 2.4 | Medium | None |
91	| `[PENDING VALIDATION]` on ~50-export merge threshold | Story 2.4 | Low | None |
92	| Batch mode gap for deepwiki pipeline | Story 2.8 review | Low | None (single-repo deepwiki doesn't use batch) |
93	| Missing `@scope/name@version` tag matching | Story 2.7 review | Low | None (scoped tags need upstream enrichment) |
94	| step-auto-scope.md aggregate complexity | Cumulative | Medium | Indirect — Epic 3's step files should avoid this pattern |
95	| Envelope optional field proliferation | Cumulative | Low | Epic 3 adds quality gate fields to TS envelope |
96	
97	All debt items are localized to deepwiki-specific code paths and do not block Epic 3.
98

115	## Epic 3 preview — Quality, Scale & Workaround Automation
116

131	## Commitments
132	
133	### Process improvements (carry into future stories)
134	
135	1. **Self-audit for internal references before review.** After the Story 2.7 and 2.8 findings, the dev agent should grep for internal identifiers (story IDs, epic numbers, internal path references) before submitting for review. This reduces review overhead and prevents internal artifacts from leaking into shipped source.
136	
137	2. **Cross-reference grep when modifying step files.** After the Story 2.3 CRITICAL finding (dangling §7 references), any step file modification that removes or renumbers sections should include a grep for cross-references in other step files that point to the changed sections.

13	# bmad-module-skill-forge — SKF Pipeline Ladder — Epic Breakdown
14	
15	## Overview
16	
17	This document provides the complete epic and story breakdown for SKF Pipeline Ladder (v1.9 deepwiki + v2.0 campaign), decomposing the requirements from the PRD and Architecture into implementable stories.
18	
19	## Requirements Inventory
20	
21	### Functional Requirements
22	
23	FR-1: System registers `deepwiki` as a pipeline alias expanding to `AN[auto] BS[auto] CS TS[min:90] EX`
24	FR-2: AN operates in auto-scope mode — analyzes source, detects skill shape, determines scope without manual configuration
25	FR-3: AN classifies repos into one of 4 skill shapes (library-API, reference-app/CLI, language-reference, stack/compose) with `unknown` fallback
26	FR-4: BS generates a brief from AN output + doc detection results, targeting 70%+ success rate (stretch 80%)
27	FR-5: User validates auto-brief via 10-line summary with progressive fallback to interactive BS
28	FR-7: Doc detection chain: gh homepageUrl → README link scanning → Pages API → docs/ folder
29	FR-8: Doc detection implemented as standalone shared module (`skf-detect-docs.py`) callable by any pipeline
30	FR-9: CS records all detected doc pages + README with content hashes in `metadata.json`
31	FR-10: AS compares current content hashes against stored hashes on every audit, flags stale skills
32	FR-11: Tiered quality gate — hard gate (zero critical/high) + soft gate (coverage percentage)
33	FR-12: Per-pipeline quality thresholds (deepwiki=90%, forge=80%), configurable via `TS[min:N]`
34	FR-13: Evidence report generated when soft gate falls back from 90% to 80%
35	FR-14: Post-compile auto-shard splits SKILL.md body exceeding 400 lines to `references/`
36	FR-15: Auto-decomposition when AN detects >500 exports or >3 packages [PENDING VALIDATION]
37	FR-16: `_known-workarounds.yaml` schema with fingerprint-to-fix pairs (versioned, validated)
38	FR-17: Pre-apply step runs before every pipeline iteration, matching fingerprints and applying fixes
39	FR-18: Registry seeded with 25 GMC campaign fingerprints
40	FR-19: Post-compile hook in CS greps feeder artifacts for corrected items, annotates with correction blocks
41	FR-20: `deepwiki <doc-url>` accepts documentation URL without repo for docs-only wiki skills
42	FR-21: deepwiki detects existing skills before generating, offers merge/alongside/skip
43	FR-22: `onboard` alias emits deprecation notice directing users to `deepwiki` (v1.9)
44	FR-23: `onboard` alias removed entirely, returns clear error with pointer (v2.0)
45	FR-24: `skf-campaign` registered as SKF's 15th workflow, invoked via `campaign`
46	FR-25: 10-stage pipeline: Setup → Strategy → Pins → Provenance → Skill Loop → Batch → Capstone → Verify → Refine → Export+Maintenance
47	FR-26: `_campaign-state.yaml` as single source of truth, human-readable YAML, survives context death
48	FR-27: `_campaign-state.yaml.bak` written before every state transition (one-deep backup)
49	FR-28: Topological sort from declared `depends_on` edges, circular dependency detection
50	FR-29: Campaign refuses to start skill whose dependencies haven't passed
51	FR-30: `campaign resume --from=<skill>` reads state, skips completed, continues
52	FR-31: `_campaign-directive.md` as file-based standing directive, read at each stage transition
53	FR-32: `campaign-validate-pins.py` validates version pins against gh releases and registries
54	FR-33: Kickoff emitter generates fresh-context handoff messages from campaign state
55	FR-34: Post-campaign report: skills completed, quality scores, findings, workarounds, doc-rot, time
56	FR-35: Machine-generated `campaign-brief.yaml` at Stage 0 for fresh-context resume
57	FR-36: `deepwiki --pin <version>` targets specific version using shared pin validation (FR-32)
58	
59	### NonFunctional Requirements
60	
61	NFR-1: Backward compatibility — no existing workflow breaks at either release
62	NFR-2: File-based state persistence — campaign state 100% recoverable from disk, no memory dependency
63	NFR-3: Shared module reusability — detect-docs, shape-detect, pre-apply, auto-shard are standalone in `src/shared/scripts/`
64	NFR-4: Graceful degradation — every auto feature has defined fallback (auto-brief→interactive, 90%→80%, etc.)
65	NFR-5: Human readability — YAML state, markdown reports, hand-recovery possible without tooling
66	NFR-6: Provenance, not generation — no feature may dilute source-grounded extraction
67	NFR-7: `-wiki` suffix enforcement — pipeline-enforced semantic metadata (coverage, quality, update semantics)
68	NFR-8: Pipeline alias as contract — alias definitions are public surface, changes are breaking
69	
70	### Additional Requirements
71	
72	From Architecture document:
73	- Campaign headless envelope: `SKF_CAMPAIGN_RESULT_JSON` at terminal step
74	- Mode flag mechanics: `[auto]` modifier passed via pipeline data context
75	- Shared module interfaces: `skf-detect-docs.py`, `skf-shape-detect.py`, `skf-preapply.py` — all via `uv run`, JSON stdout, exit codes 0/1/2
76	- Campaign state schema: JSON Schema validation at `assets/campaign-state-schema.json`
77	- Step file patterns: stateless interpreters, read-backup-modify-write for campaign
78	- Per-pipeline threshold lookup table in TS first step
79	- Kickoff emitter template contract at `templates/kickoff-template.md`
80	- Pre-apply registry merge: shared seed + project-local, ephemeral merge at invocation
81	- Implementation sequence: shared modules → AN/BS auto → CS/AS enhancements → TS gates → deepwiki alias → campaign schema → campaign steps
82	- SS active_version manifest-state bugfix (v1.9 scope per brainstorming BH-8)
83	
84	### UX Design Requirements
85	
86	Not applicable — CLI/agent tool with no UI surface.
87

89	
90	| FR | Epic | Description |
91	|----|------|-------------|
92	| FR-3 | Epic 1 | Skill-shape detection (shared module) |

94	| FR-8 | Epic 1 | Doc detection as shared module |

99	| FR-4 | Epic 2 | Auto-brief generation |
100	| FR-5 | Epic 2 | Auto-brief validation + progressive fallback |
101	| FR-15 | Epic 2 | Auto-decomposition for massive repos |
102	| FR-20 | Epic 2 | Docs-only path |
103	| FR-21 | Epic 2 | Coexistence detection |
104	| FR-36 | Epic 2 | Version pinning |
105	| FR-22 | Epic 2 | onboard deprecation (v1.9) |
106	| FR-11 | Epic 3 | Tiered quality gate |
107	| FR-12 | Epic 3 | Per-pipeline quality thresholds |
108	| FR-13 | Epic 3 | Evidence report on fallback |
109	| FR-14 | Epic 3 | Auto-shard at 400-line ceiling |
110	| FR-16 | Epic 3 | Workaround registry schema |

118	| FR-28 | Epic 4 | Dependency tracking |
119	| FR-29 | Epic 4 | Dependency enforcement |
120	| FR-30 | Epic 4 | Resume from any skill |
121	| FR-31 | Epic 4 | Standing directive |
122	| FR-32 | Epic 4 | Pin validation |
123	| FR-33 | Epic 4 | Kickoff emitter |
124	| FR-34 | Epic 4 | Campaign report |
125	| FR-35 | Epic 4 | Campaign-brief.yaml |
126	| FR-23 | Epic 4 | onboard removal (v2.0) |
127	
128	## Epic List
129	
130	### Epic 1: Source Intelligence Foundation
131	SKF understands sources better — detects documentation URLs, classifies repo shapes, tracks upstream doc changes, flags stale skills. Creates the shared modules that all downstream epics depend on.
132	**FRs covered:** FR-3, FR-7, FR-8, FR-9, FR-10 + SS active_version bugfix
133	
134	### Epic 2: deepwiki — Zero-Ceremony Skill Creation
135	Run `deepwiki <repo-url>` and get a 90%-coverage wiki skill in 3-5 minutes. Also works for doc URLs, specific versions, and detects existing skills before overwriting. Includes v1.9 documentation and onboard deprecation.
136	**FRs covered:** FR-1, FR-2, FR-4, FR-5, FR-15, FR-20, FR-21, FR-36, FR-22
137	
138	### Epic 3: Quality, Scale & Workaround Automation
139	Higher quality skills with transparent quality gates, automatic handling of large outputs, and no more re-discovering workarounds across pipeline runs. Core improvements that benefit all pipelines.
140	**FRs covered:** FR-11, FR-12, FR-13, FR-14, FR-16, FR-17, FR-18, FR-19
141	
142	### Epic 4: Campaign Orchestration (v2.0)
143	Orchestrate 15+ coordinated skills across multiple sessions with dependency tracking, file-based state, and resume from any skill. Includes v2.0 documentation and onboard removal.
144	**FRs covered:** FR-24, FR-25, FR-26, FR-27, FR-28, FR-29, FR-30, FR-31, FR-32, FR-33, FR-34, FR-35, FR-23
145	# SKF Pipeline Ladder — Epic 1 & 2 Story Breakdown
146	
147	---
148	
149	## Epic 1: Source Intelligence Foundation
150	
151	**Goal:** SKF understands sources better — detects documentation URLs, classifies repo shapes, tracks upstream doc changes, flags stale skills.
152	
153	**FRs:** FR-3, FR-7, FR-8, FR-9, FR-10 + SS active_version bugfix
154	
155	---
156	
157	### Story 1.1: Skill-shape detection shared module
158	
159	As a pipeline author,
160	I want a standalone shape-detection script that classifies repos into known skill shapes,
161	So that AN auto-scope, BS auto-brief, and TS threshold selection can all consume the same classification without duplicating heuristics.
162	
163	**Acceptance Criteria:**
164	
165	**Given** a repo URL and one or more manifest paths (package.json, pyproject.toml, Cargo.toml)
166	**When** `uv run python src/shared/scripts/skf-shape-detect.py --repo-url <url> --manifests <paths>` is invoked
167	**Then** stdout contains a JSON object `{shape, signals[], confidence, export_count, package_count}`
168	**And** `shape` is one of `library-API`, `reference-app`, `language-reference`, `stack-compose`
169	**And** exit code is `0`
170	
171	**Given** a repo whose manifests and structure do not match any known shape
172	**When** shape detection is invoked
173	**Then** `shape` is `unknown`
174	**And** exit code is `1`
175	
176	**Given** an invalid repo URL or missing manifest paths
177	**When** shape detection is invoked
178	**Then** stderr contains `{"error": "...", "code": "ERROR_TYPE"}`
179	**And** exit code is `2`
180	
181	**Given** the test suite at `test/test-skf-shape-detect.py`
182	**When** `uv run pytest test/test-skf-shape-detect.py` is executed
183	**Then** all tests pass
184	**And** the test file is registered in `package.json` test:python script
185	
186	**Technical Notes:**
187	- File: `src/shared/scripts/skf-shape-detect.py`
188	- Test: `test/test-skf-shape-detect.py`
189	- Follow existing shared script pattern (skf-preflight.py, skf-detect-language.py): args via argparse, JSON stdout, stderr errors, exit codes 0/1/2
190	- Signals array contains the evidence that drove the classification (e.g., `["has_package_json", "exports_count_gt_50", "no_bin_field"]`)
191	- Confidence is a float 0.0-1.0
192	
193	---
194	
195	### Story 1.2: Doc detection chain shared module
196	
197	As a pipeline author,
198	I want a standalone doc-detection script that discovers documentation URLs through multiple methods,
199	So that BS, CS, AS, and campaign workflows can all locate a repo's docs without duplicating detection logic.
200	
201	**Acceptance Criteria:**
202	
203	**Given** a repo URL with a `homepageUrl` set on GitHub
204	**When** `uv run python src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-docs.py --repo-url <url>` is invoked
205	**Then** stdout contains a JSON array including an entry with `detected_via: "homepageUrl"`
206	**And** exit code is `0`
207	
208	**Given** a repo whose README contains documentation links
209	**When** doc detection is invoked
210	**Then** the output array includes entries with `detected_via: "readme_link"`
211	**And** each entry has `url`, `content_hash` (SHA-256 hex lowercase), and `content_type`
212	
213	**Given** a repo with a GitHub Pages site
214	**When** doc detection is invoked without `--skip-pages-api`
215	**Then** the output array includes an entry with `detected_via: "pages_api"`
216	
217	**Given** a repo with a `docs/` folder containing markdown files
218	**When** doc detection is invoked
219	**Then** the output array includes entries with `detected_via: "docs_folder"`
220	
221	**Given** a repo whose README links to a CHANGELOG.md or migration guide
222	**When** doc detection is invoked
223	**Then** changelogs and migration guides are excluded from the output array
224	
225	**Given** all four detection methods find zero documentation sources
226	**When** doc detection is invoked
227	**Then** stdout contains an empty JSON array `[]`
228	**And** exit code is `1`
229	
230	**Given** a `--local-path` argument alongside `--repo-url`
231	**When** doc detection is invoked
232	**Then** docs/ folder detection uses the local path instead of cloning
233	
234	**Given** the test suite at `test/test-skf-detect-docs.py`
235	**When** `uv run pytest test/test-skf-detect-docs.py` is executed
236	**Then** all tests pass
237	**And** the test file is registered in `package.json` test:python script
238	
239	**Technical Notes:**
240	- File: `src/shared/scripts/skf-detect-docs.py`
241	- Test: `test/test-skf-detect-docs.py`
242	- All four detection methods are attempted and results aggregated (not short-circuited)
243	- Output schema: `[{url: string, detected_via: string, content_hash: string, content_type: string}]`
244	- Content hashes: SHA-256, hex-encoded, lowercase
245	- Exit codes: 0 (found >=1), 1 (none found), 2 (error)
246	
247	---
248	
249	### Story 1.3: Doc tracking at compile time
250	
251	As a skill maintainer,
252	I want CS to record detected documentation pages and README with content hashes at compile time,
253	So that future audits can detect when upstream docs have changed since the skill was compiled.
254	
255	**Acceptance Criteria:**
256	
257	**Given** a skill brief with a repo URL and doc detection results available from the pipeline
258	**When** CS compile runs and reaches the doc-sources step
259	**Then** `metadata.json` contains a `doc_sources` array with entries `{url, detected_via, content_hash, recorded_at}`
260	**And** `content_hash` values are SHA-256 hex lowercase
261	**And** `recorded_at` is an ISO-8601 timestamp with timezone
262	
263	**Given** a compile where doc detection found zero external docs
264	**When** CS compile runs
265	**Then** `metadata.json` `doc_sources` still contains at least one entry for the repo's `README.md`
266	**And** the README entry has `detected_via: "readme_always"`
267	
268	**Given** a compile where doc detection found three external doc pages
269	**When** CS compile completes
270	**Then** `doc_sources` contains exactly four entries (three detected + README)
271	
272	**Given** doc_sources already exist in metadata.json from a prior compile
273	**When** CS recompiles the same skill
274	**Then** `doc_sources` is fully replaced with current detection results (not appended to stale data)
275	
276	**Technical Notes:**
277	- New step file: `src/skf-create-skill/references/step-doc-sources.md`
278	- Step invokes `skf-detect-docs.py` if doc detection was not already run upstream in the pipeline
279	- Writes to the same `metadata.json` that CS already produces
280	- README.md is always tracked as a baseline regardless of detection results
281	
282	---
283	
284	### Story 1.4: Doc drift detection in audit
285	
286	As a skill maintainer,
287	I want AS to compare current documentation hashes against the hashes stored at compile time,
288	So that I know which upstream docs have changed and can decide whether the skill needs updating.
289	
290	**Acceptance Criteria:**
291	
292	**Given** a skill with `doc_sources` recorded in `metadata.json` and one tracked doc has changed since compile
293	**When** `@Ferris AS` runs against that skill
294	**Then** the audit report includes a doc drift section listing the changed doc with `{url, old_hash, new_hash, detected_at}`
295	**And** the drift is reported alongside (not instead of) source code drift
296	
297	**Given** a skill with `doc_sources` and no tracked docs have changed
298	**When** AS runs
299	**Then** the doc drift section reports "No documentation drift detected"
300	**And** no false positives appear
301	
302	**Given** a skill compiled before Story 1.3 (no `doc_sources` in metadata.json)
303	**When** AS runs
304	**Then** the doc drift step is skipped gracefully with a note: "No doc_sources recorded — skip doc drift check"
305	**And** the rest of the audit proceeds normally
306	
307	**Given** a tracked doc URL that returns a network error during audit
308	**When** AS attempts to fetch and hash the doc
309	**Then** the drift report marks that URL as `"status": "fetch_failed"` instead of reporting false drift
310	**And** the audit does not abort
311	
312	**Technical Notes:**
313	- New step file: `src/skf-audit-skill/references/step-doc-drift.md`
314	- Step re-fetches each tracked URL, computes SHA-256, compares against stored `content_hash`
315	- Drift report format: per-doc `{url, old_hash, new_hash, detected_at}` — human-readable markdown output
316	- No content stored — only hashes compared
317	
318	---
319	
320	### Story 1.5: SS active_version manifest-state bugfix
321	
322	As a stack skill user,
323	I want SS manifest operations to keep `active_version` consistent after every operation,
324	So that stranded active_version state does not cause downstream workflows to reference the wrong skill version.
325	
326	**Acceptance Criteria:**
327	
328	**Given** a manifest with multiple versions where `active_version` points to version A
329	**When** SS performs a set operation that activates version B
330	**Then** `active_version` is updated to version B
331	**And** version A's status is archived
332	**And** no version other than B has active status
333	
334	**Given** a manifest where `active_version` was pre-advanced (advanced before the set operation ran)
335	**When** SS set runs
336	**Then** the prior genuinely-active version is archived
337	**And** `active_version` reflects the correct current version
338	**And** no stranded active state remains
339	
340	**Given** the existing test suite at `test/test-skf-manifest-ops.py`
341	**When** `uv run pytest test/test-skf-manifest-ops.py` is executed after the fix
342	**Then** all existing tests pass (no regressions)
343	**And** any new regression test covering the stranded-state scenario passes
344	
345	**Technical Notes:**
346	- Fix location: `src/shared/scripts/skf-manifest-ops.py`
347	- Test location: `test/test-skf-manifest-ops.py`
348	- This is a bugfix, not a new feature — the stranded state was identified during brainstorming (BH-8)
349	- Verify the fix does not break compose-mode SS flows that rely on manifest operations
350	
351	---
352	---
353	
354	## Epic 2: deepwiki — Zero-Ceremony Skill Creation
355	
356	**Goal:** Run `deepwiki <repo-url>` and get a 90%-coverage wiki skill in 3-5 minutes.
357	
358	**FRs:** FR-1, FR-2, FR-4, FR-5, FR-15, FR-20, FR-21, FR-36, FR-22
359	
360	---
361	
362	### Story 2.1: Auto-scope mode for AN
363	
364	As a deepwiki user,
365	I want AN to automatically scope a repo using shape detection and export surface analysis,
366	So that the deepwiki pipeline produces a scope without requiring manual input.
367	
368	**Acceptance Criteria:**
369	
370	**Given** a repo URL with a standard package manifest (package.json, pyproject.toml, or Cargo.toml)
371	**When** AN runs with the `[auto]` flag passed via pipeline context
372	**Then** AN invokes `skf-shape-detect.py` and produces a scope from the shape + export surface
373	**And** no user input is required
374	**And** the scope output matches AN's existing scope schema
375	
376	**Given** a repo where shape detection returns `unknown` (exit code 1)
377	**When** AN runs in auto mode
378	**Then** AN falls back to the interactive scope definition step
379	**And** the user is informed: "Auto-scope could not classify this repo — switching to interactive mode"
380	
381	**Given** AN is invoked without the `[auto]` flag
382	**When** the user runs `@Ferris AN`
383	**Then** AN follows its existing interactive flow unchanged
384	**And** the auto-scope step is not loaded
385	
386	**Given** AN auto-scope produces a scope for a repo with 200 exports
387	**When** the scope is passed downstream to BS
388	**Then** the scope includes export count, shape, and detected signals from shape detection
389	
390	**Technical Notes:**
391	- New step files: `src/skf-analyze-source/references/step-auto-scope.md`, `src/skf-analyze-source/references/step-shape-detect.md`
392	- `[auto]` flag is a pipeline data context modifier, handled by existing pipeline infrastructure
393	- AN's first step checks for `[auto]` flag and routes to `step-auto-scope.md` or the existing interactive step
394	- Depends on Story 1.1 (skf-shape-detect.py)
395	
396	---
397	
398	### Story 2.2: Auto-brief generation for BS
399	
400	As a deepwiki user,
401	I want BS to automatically generate a skill brief from AN auto-scope output and doc detection results,
402	So that the deepwiki pipeline produces a brief without requiring manual input.
403	
404	**Acceptance Criteria:**
405	
406	**Given** AN auto-scope output with shape, export count, and scope definition, plus doc detection results
407	**When** BS runs with the `[auto]` flag passed via pipeline context
408	**Then** BS generates a structured brief matching the existing BS schema
409	**And** the brief targets `scope_type: full-library` or `scope_type: public-api` based on the scope
410	**And** the brief includes detected doc URLs from `skf-detect-docs.py` output
411	
412	**Given** AN auto-scope output but zero detected documentation sources
413	**When** BS runs in auto mode
414	**Then** the brief is still generated (docs are enrichment, not a requirement)
415	**And** the brief's documentation section is empty or contains only the README reference
416	
417	**Given** BS is invoked without the `[auto]` flag
418	**When** the user runs `@Ferris BS`
419	**Then** BS follows its existing interactive flow unchanged
420	
421	**Given** a successful auto-brief generation
422	**When** the brief YAML is written
423	**Then** it passes validation against the existing brief schema (`skf-validate-brief-schema.py`)
424	
425	**Technical Notes:**
426	- New step file: `src/skf-brief-skill/references/step-auto-brief.md`
427	- BS's first step checks for `[auto]` flag and routes to `step-auto-brief.md` or the existing interactive `gather-intent.md`
428	- Depends on Story 2.1 (AN auto-scope output) and Story 1.2 (skf-detect-docs.py)
429	- Auto-brief targets full-library for library-API shapes, public-api for reference-app shapes
430	
431	---
432	
433	### Story 2.3: Auto-brief validation with progressive fallback
434	
435	As a deepwiki user,
436	I want to review a 10-line summary of the auto-generated brief before CS runs,
437	So that I can approve, edit, or reject it — and rejection falls back to interactive BS with pre-populated fields.
438	
439	**Acceptance Criteria:**
440	
441	**Given** BS auto-brief has been generated
442	**When** the validation step presents the summary to the user
443	**Then** the summary contains exactly: shape, scope type, export count, doc sources, quality target (10 lines)
444	**And** the user is offered three actions: `[A]pprove`, `[E]dit`, `[R]eject`
445	
446	**Given** the user selects `[A]pprove`
447	**When** validation completes
448	**Then** the pipeline continues to CS with the auto-generated brief
449	**And** no further brief interaction occurs
450	
451	**Given** the user selects `[E]dit`
452	**When** validation handles the edit
453	**Then** the user can modify specific fields of the brief
454	**And** the modified brief is re-validated against the brief schema
455	**And** the pipeline continues to CS with the edited brief
456	
457	**Given** the user selects `[R]eject`
458	**When** validation handles the rejection
459	**Then** the pipeline does not proceed to CS
460	**And** interactive BS loads with fields pre-populated from the partial auto-detection results
461	**And** the user is informed: "Falling back to interactive brief — fields pre-populated from auto-detection"
462	
463	**Given** the pipeline is running in headless mode
464	**When** the validation step is reached
465	**Then** `[A]pprove` is auto-selected (headless default)
466	**And** the pipeline continues without pausing
467	
468	**Technical Notes:**
469	- New step file: `src/skf-brief-skill/references/step-auto-validate.md`
470	- This step runs between auto-brief generation and CS invocation
471	- Pre-population on reject: shape, scope type, doc URLs, and export list carry into the interactive flow
472	- Headless gate type: auto-proceed with `[A]` per headless-gate-convention.md
473	
474	---
475	
476	### Story 2.4: Auto-decomposition for massive repos
477	
478	As a deepwiki user targeting a large repo,
479	I want AN to automatically recommend multi-skill decomposition when the repo exceeds complexity thresholds,
480	So that massive repos produce multiple focused skills instead of one unwieldy skill.
481	
482	**Acceptance Criteria:**
483	
484	**Given** a repo where AN auto-scope detects more than 500 exports
485	**When** auto-scope runs
486	**Then** AN recommends a multi-skill decomposition
487	**And** the recommendation includes suggested skill boundaries
488	
489	**Given** a repo where AN auto-scope detects more than 3 packages (monorepo/workspace)
490	**When** auto-scope runs
491	**Then** AN recommends decomposition with one skill per package (or logical grouping)
492	
493	**Given** a repo with 200 exports and 1 package
494	**When** auto-scope runs
495	**Then** no decomposition is recommended
496	**And** a single scope is produced
497	
498	**Given** AN recommends decomposition in the deepwiki pipeline
499	**When** the pipeline proceeds
500	**Then** BS creates N briefs (one per recommended skill)
501	**And** CS compiles N skills
502	**And** SS auto-composes a stack from the N skills
503	**And** the user never manually chooses decomposition boundaries
504	
505	**Given** the decomposition thresholds
506	**When** reviewing the implementation
507	**Then** the 500-export and 3-package thresholds are marked `[PENDING VALIDATION]` in step-auto-scope.md
508	
509	**Technical Notes:**
510	- Enhancement within `src/skf-analyze-source/references/step-auto-scope.md` (same file as Story 2.1)
511	- Thresholds are constants marked `[PENDING VALIDATION]` — expected to be tuned after empirical testing
512	- Multi-brief flow leverages existing CS `--batch` and SS compose-mode
513	- GAP-2 from architecture: the multi-brief to multi-CS to SS compose chain is step-file-level orchestration within the deepwiki pipeline
514	
515	---
516	
517	### Story 2.5: Docs-only deepwiki path
518	
519	As a developer who wants a skill for a documentation site (no repo),
520	I want to run `deepwiki <doc-url>` with just a documentation URL,
521	So that I get a wiki skill built entirely from documentation content.
522	
523	**Acceptance Criteria:**
524	
525	**Given** a documentation URL (e.g., `https://docs.example.com`) without a repo URL
526	**When** `deepwiki <doc-url>` is invoked
527	**Then** the doc detection chain runs against the provided URL as the primary source
528	**And** a wiki skill is created with `source_type: docs-only` in the brief
529	
530	**Given** a docs-only invocation
531	**When** BS generates the brief
532	**Then** `source_type` is set to `docs-only`
533	**And** the brief does not require a repo URL
534	**And** the brief references the documentation URL as the primary source
535	
536	**Given** a docs-only invocation where the URL is unreachable
537	**When** the pipeline starts
538	**Then** the pipeline halts with a clear error: "Documentation URL unreachable: <url>"
539	**And** exit code reflects the error
540	
541	**Given** a standard repo URL (e.g., `https://github.com/org/repo`)
542	**When** `deepwiki` is invoked with that URL
543	**Then** the standard repo-based deepwiki flow runs (not the docs-only path)
544	
545	**Technical Notes:**
546	- Leverages the existing `source_type: docs-only` path already supported by BS
547	- The detection of URL type (repo vs docs) happens at pipeline entry — AN's first step or the pipeline alias parser
548	- Doc detection chain (`skf-detect-docs.py`) runs against the URL; the URL itself becomes the primary doc source
549	- Depends on Story 1.2 (skf-detect-docs.py)
550	
551	---
552	
553	### Story 2.6: Coexistence detection
554	
555	As a deepwiki user,
556	I want deepwiki to detect existing skills for the same repo/library before generating a new one,
557	So that I can choose to create alongside, merge, or skip — and never silently overwrite an existing skill.
558	
559	**Acceptance Criteria:**
560	
561	**Given** existing skills for library `foo` already present in the forge output folder
562	**When** `deepwiki` targets the same library `foo`
563	**Then** the user is informed: "Existing skill(s) found for foo: [list]"
564	**And** the user is offered three actions: `[A]longside` (create wiki variant), `[M]erge` (update existing), `[S]kip`
565	
566	**Given** the user selects `[A]longside`
567	**When** the pipeline proceeds
568	**Then** a new wiki skill is created with a `-wiki` suffix
569	**And** the existing skill is not modified
570	
571	**Given** the user selects `[M]erge`
572	**When** the pipeline proceeds
573	**Then** the existing skill is updated through the US (Update Skill) workflow
574	**And** the wiki detection data enriches the existing skill
575	
576	**Given** the user selects `[S]kip`
577	**When** the pipeline handles the skip
578	**Then** no skill is created or modified for that library
579	**And** the pipeline reports "Skipped: existing skill for foo"
580	
581	**Given** no existing skills for the targeted library
582	**When** deepwiki runs
583	**Then** coexistence detection completes silently
584	**And** the pipeline proceeds directly to skill creation
585	
586	**Given** the pipeline is running in headless mode
587	**When** coexistence is detected
588	**Then** `[A]longside` is auto-selected (headless default — never silently overwrite)
589	
590	**Technical Notes:**
591	- Detection uses `skf-skill-inventory.py` to find existing skills matching the repo URL or package name
592	- Coexistence check runs early in the deepwiki pipeline, before AN auto-scope
593	- Headless default is `[A]longside` (safest — creates new, preserves existing)
594	
595	---
596	
597	### Story 2.7: Version pinning for deepwiki
598	
599	As a developer targeting a specific library version,
600	I want to run `deepwiki <repo-url> --pin <version>` to generate a skill for that exact version,
601	So that my wiki skill reflects a known release rather than latest HEAD.
602	
603	**Acceptance Criteria:**
604	
605	**Given** `deepwiki <repo-url> --pin v2.0.0` where `v2.0.0` is a valid semver tag on the repo
606	**When** the pipeline runs
607	**Then** AN scopes against the pinned version's code
608	**And** CS records the pinned version in metadata.json provenance
609	**And** the generated skill reflects the v2.0.0 API surface
610	
611	**Given** `deepwiki <repo-url> --pin v99.0.0` where `v99.0.0` does not exist
612	**When** the pipeline starts
613	**Then** the pipeline halts with a clear error listing the invalid pin
614	**And** the error includes suggested corrections (e.g., available tags matching `v99.*`)
615	
616	**Given** `deepwiki <repo-url>` without `--pin`
617	**When** the pipeline runs
618	**Then** the latest release tag is used as the default pin
619	**And** metadata.json records the resolved version
620	
621	**Given** `deepwiki <repo-url> --pin main` with a branch name instead of a tag
622	**When** the pipeline runs
623	**Then** the branch name is accepted as a valid pin
624	**And** AN scopes against the branch HEAD
625	
626	**Given** pin validation logic
627	**When** comparing with campaign pin validation (FR-32)
628	**Then** both use the same shared validation module (no duplicate implementation)
629	
630	**Technical Notes:**
631	- Pin validation reuses the shared `campaign-validate-pins.py` logic (or a shared extraction thereof)
632	- `--pin` is consumed by AN's first step, passed downstream to CS for provenance recording
633	- Default (no --pin): resolve latest release tag via `gh release list --limit 1` or equivalent
634	- Accepts semver tags, git tags, and branch names
635	
636	---
637	
638	### Story 2.8: deepwiki pipeline alias + onboard deprecation
639	
640	As a user,
641	I want to type `deepwiki <repo-url>` and have the full wiki-skill pipeline run automatically,
642	So that I get a high-quality wiki skill with zero ceremony.
643	
644	**Acceptance Criteria:**
645	
646	**Given** the forger SKILL.md pipeline alias table
647	**When** reviewing the registered aliases
648	**Then** `deepwiki` is listed and expands to `AN[auto] BS[auto] CS TS[min:90] EX`
649	
650	**Given** `deepwiki <repo-url>` invoked by the user
651	**When** the pipeline runs
652	**Then** AN runs in auto mode, BS runs in auto mode, CS compiles, TS tests at 90% threshold, EX exports
653	**And** each workflow receives the `[auto]` flag through pipeline data context
654	
655	**Given** `[auto]` mode flag in a pipeline sequence
656	**When** the pipeline infrastructure parses `AN[auto]`
657	**Then** the `auto` flag is set in the workflow data context for AN
658	**And** AN's first step reads the flag and routes to auto-scope
659	
660	**Given** `onboard <repo-url>` invoked by the user
661	**When** the pipeline starts
662	**Then** a deprecation notice is displayed: "onboard is deprecated — use deepwiki instead"
663	**And** migration guidance is shown on every invocation
664	**And** the onboard pipeline runs at 80% threshold (existing behavior)
665	**And** the pipeline completes despite the deprecation notice
666	
667	**Given** the pipeline alias table in `src/skf-forger/SKILL.md`
668	**When** reviewing the Pipeline Mode section
669	**Then** `deepwiki` appears in the alias expansion list alongside `forge`, `forge-quick`, `onboard`, `maintain`
670	
671	**Technical Notes:**
672	- Registration: add `deepwiki` to the alias expansion table in `src/skf-forger/SKILL.md` Pipeline Mode section
673	- `[auto]` is a new modifier type alongside existing `[min:N]` — pipeline infrastructure already handles bracket modifiers
674	- Onboard deprecation: modify the `onboard` alias handling to prepend the deprecation notice, keep existing behavior at 80%
675	- No new pipeline engine — deepwiki uses existing left-to-right chaining, headless auto-proceed, and circuit breaker mechanics
676	
677	---
678	
679	### Story 2.9: v1.9 documentation
680	
681	As a user or contributor,
682	I want documentation that explains the deepwiki workflow and the onboard deprecation,
683	So that I can discover and use deepwiki without reading source code.
684	
685	**Acceptance Criteria:**
686	
687	**Given** `docs/workflows.md`
688	**When** reviewing the Pipeline Aliases table
689	**Then** `deepwiki` is listed with its expansion `AN[auto] BS[auto] CS TS[min:90] EX`
690	**And** `onboard` is marked `(deprecated — use deepwiki)`
691	
692	**Given** `docs/deepwiki.md`
693	**When** the file is reviewed
694	**Then** it contains: purpose, invocation syntax, supported input types (repo URL, doc URL, --pin), pipeline stages, expected output, and timing guidance (3-5 minutes)
695	**And** it links to workflows.md for pipeline mechanics and concepts.md for terminology
696	
697	**Given** `docs/bmad-synergy.md`
698	**When** the file is reviewed
699	**Then** it includes a "SKF Without BMM" section explaining that deepwiki is the fast-path for users not using the full BMAD Method
700	**And** deepwiki is positioned as the recommended entry point for standalone SKF usage
701	
702	**Given** `docs/workflows.md` Feature Workflows section
703	**When** reviewing workflow listings
704	**Then** the `onboard` alias entry includes the deprecation notice directing users to `deepwiki`
705	
706	**Technical Notes:**
707	- `docs/workflows.md`: update Pipeline Aliases table, add deepwiki to Feature Workflows, mark onboard deprecated
708	- `docs/deepwiki.md`: new dedicated guide — concise, focused on invocation and expected results
709	- `docs/bmad-synergy.md`: add "SKF Without BMM" section with deepwiki as the recommended fast-path
710	- Follow existing docs style: frontmatter with title/description, link to related docs, no inline version stamps
711	
712	# Epic 3 & 4 — Story Drafts
713	
714	## Epic 3: Quality, Scale & Workaround Automation
715	
716	Goal: Higher quality skills with transparent quality gates, automatic handling of large outputs, and no more re-discovering workarounds.
717	
718	FRs: FR-11, FR-12, FR-13, FR-14, FR-16, FR-17, FR-18, FR-19
719	
720	---
721	
722	### Story 3.1: Tiered quality gate — hard gate
723	
724	As a skill author,
725	I want the test pipeline to block on unresolved critical/high findings before scoring,
726	So that no skill with serious defects can pass regardless of its coverage percentage.
727	
728	**Acceptance Criteria:**
729	
730	**Given** a skill with one or more unresolved critical-severity findings
731	**When** TS runs the hard gate check (step-hard-gate.md) before scoring
732	**Then** the pipeline blocks with a clear message listing each critical/high finding
733	**And** no coverage score is computed or reported
734	
735	**Given** a skill with one or more unresolved high-severity findings
736	**When** TS runs the hard gate check
737	**Then** the pipeline blocks with the same behavior as critical findings
738	
739	**Given** a skill with only medium and low severity findings
740	**When** TS runs the hard gate check
741	**Then** the hard gate passes and the pipeline proceeds to the soft gate (coverage scoring)
742	**And** medium/low findings are documented in the gap report but do not block
743	
744	**Deliverables:**
745	- `src/skf-test-skill/references/step-hard-gate.md`
746	
747	---
748	
749	### Story 3.2: Per-pipeline quality thresholds
750	
751	As a pipeline operator,
752	I want each pipeline alias to declare its own default quality threshold,
753	So that deepwiki skills are held to 90% while forge skills default to 80%.
754	
755	**Acceptance Criteria:**
756	
757	**Given** a TS invocation within the deepwiki pipeline and no explicit override
758	**When** TS looks up the threshold
759	**Then** the default threshold is 90%
760	
761	**Given** a TS invocation within the forge pipeline and no explicit override
762	**When** TS looks up the threshold
763	**Then** the default threshold is 80%
764	
765	**Given** a TS invocation with the `TS[min:85]` override syntax
766	**When** TS resolves the threshold
767	**Then** the override value of 85% is used instead of the pipeline default
768	**And** the source of the threshold (override vs default) is logged
769	
770	**Given** any pipeline and any threshold configuration
771	**When** TS runs
772	**Then** the hard gate (Story 3.1) is enforced before the soft gate regardless of threshold value
773	
774	**Deliverables:**
775	- Modified TS first step to include threshold lookup table: `{deepwiki: 90, forge: 80, forge-quick: 80, campaign: 90}`
776	
777	---
778	
779	### Story 3.3: Evidence report on threshold fallback
780	
781	As a skill author,
782	I want a written evidence report when the soft gate falls back from its target threshold to the 80% floor,
783	So that the quality compromise is documented and never silent.
784	
785	**Acceptance Criteria:**
786	
787	**Given** a skill that scores 82% against a 90% target and remediation has been attempted
788	**When** TS falls back from the 90% target to the 80% floor
789	**Then** an evidence report is written to `forge-data/{skill-name}/{version}/evidence-report-fallback.md`
790	**And** the report contains: attempted threshold, achieved score, specific findings preventing the higher threshold, remediation steps attempted, and the final accepted threshold
791	
792	**Given** a fallback occurs
793	**When** the pipeline completes
794	**Then** the fallback is visible in the pipeline output (not buried in the report alone)
795	**And** the pipeline output includes the path to the evidence report
796	
797	**Given** a skill that meets its target threshold without fallback
798	**When** TS completes scoring
799	**Then** no evidence report is generated
800	
801	---
802	
803	### Story 3.4: Auto-shard at 400-line ceiling
804	
805	As a skill author,
806	I want SKILL.md bodies exceeding 400 lines to be automatically sharded into references,
807	So that skills stay within the 500-line skill-check ceiling with comfortable headroom.
808	
809	**Acceptance Criteria:**
810	
811	**Given** a compiled SKILL.md with a body exceeding 400 lines
812	**When** the auto-shard step runs post-compile
813	**Then** navigational catalogs and deep-dive sections are moved to `references/`
814	**And** the SKILL.md body is reduced to under 400 lines
815	**And** hallucination guards (T1 markers, confidence annotations) remain inline in SKILL.md
816	
817	**Given** a compiled SKILL.md with a body of 350 lines
818	**When** the auto-shard step runs post-compile
819	**Then** no sharding is triggered and SKILL.md is unchanged
820	
821	**Given** a sharded SKILL.md
822	**When** the resulting skill is validated by skill-check
823	**Then** the skill passes the 500-line ceiling check
824	**And** cross-references between SKILL.md and sharded reference files are intact
825	
826	**Deliverables:**
827	- `src/skf-create-skill/references/step-auto-shard.md`
828	
829	---
830	
831	### Story 3.5: Pre-apply workaround registry
832	
833	As a pipeline operator,
834	I want known workarounds to be automatically applied before each pipeline iteration,
835	So that previously-solved issues are not re-discovered and manually fixed each time.
836	
837	**Acceptance Criteria:**
838	
839	**Given** a `_known-workarounds.yaml` registry with a fingerprint matching content in the target directory
840	**When** `skf-preapply.py --target-dir <path>` runs
841	**Then** the matching fix is applied to the target content
842	**And** the applied fix is logged to `forge-data/{skill-name}/{version}/preapply-log.json`
843	
844	**Given** a registry with no fingerprints matching the target content
845	**When** `skf-preapply.py` runs
846	**Then** no fixes are applied and the log records zero matches
847	
848	**Given** both a shared seed registry and a project-local registry with a colliding fingerprint
849	**When** `skf-preapply.py --target-dir <path> --local-registry <path>` runs
850	**Then** the project-local fix wins on collision
851	**And** the shared seed is loaded first, project-local merged on top
852	
853	**Given** a new workaround discovered during a pipeline run
854	**When** it is recorded
855	**Then** it is appended to the project-local registry only, never to the shared seed
856	
857	**Given** a fingerprint entry with `regex: true`
858	**When** matching is performed
859	**Then** regex matching is used instead of the default substring matching
860	
861	**Deliverables:**
862	- `src/shared/scripts/skf-preapply.py`
863	- `src/shared/_known-workarounds.yaml` (schema: version 1, workarounds array with fingerprint, fix, source, severity, description)
864	- Shared seed with 25 GMC campaign fingerprints
865	- `test/test-skf-preapply.py`
866	
867	---
868	
869	### Story 3.6: Doc-rot correction hooks
870	
871	As a skill author,
872	I want corrected items in feeder artifacts to be automatically annotated post-compile,
873	So that doc-rot corrections are surfaced deterministically without relying on AI judgment.
874	
875	**Acceptance Criteria:**
876	
877	**Given** a feeder artifact containing corrected content (identifiable via grep patterns)
878	**When** the doc-rot hook fires post-compile
879	**Then** correction blocks (`## CORRECTION`) are annotated in the compiled output
880	
881	**Given** the doc-rot hook
882	**When** it performs matching
883	**Then** matching is grep-based and deterministic (no AI judgment involved)
884	
885	**Given** a standalone deepwiki pipeline run (not a campaign)
886	**When** CS completes compilation
887	**Then** the doc-rot hook fires (it is not campaign-only)
888	
889	**Given** a feeder artifact with no corrected content
890	**When** the doc-rot hook fires
891	**Then** no correction blocks are added and the output is unchanged
892	
893	**Deliverables:**
894	- `src/skf-create-skill/references/step-doc-rot.md`
895	
896	---
897	
898	## Epic 4: Campaign Orchestration (v2.0)
899	
900	Goal: Orchestrate 15+ coordinated skills across multiple sessions with dependency tracking, file-based state, and resume.
901	
902	FRs: FR-24 through FR-35, FR-23
903	
904	---
905	
906	### Story 4.1: Campaign workflow scaffolding + state schema
907	
908	As a campaign operator,
909	I want the skf-campaign workflow directory and state schema to exist,
910	So that campaign orchestration has a well-defined entry point and validated state format.
911	
912	**Acceptance Criteria:**
913	
914	**Given** the SKF source tree
915	**When** the campaign workflow is scaffolded
916	**Then** `src/skf-campaign/` exists with: SKILL.md, manifest.yaml, references/, assets/, templates/, scripts/
917	
918	**Given** the campaign state schema at `assets/campaign-state-schema.json`
919	**When** a `_campaign-state.yaml` file is validated against it
920	**Then** the schema enforces: campaign (name, started_at, last_updated, current_stage, directive_path, quality_gate, health_findings_queue), skills (name, status, depends_on, tier, pin, brief_path, skill_path, quality_score, workarounds_applied, started_at, completed_at), dependency_graph (execution_order, circular_deps_detected)
921	
922	**Given** any operation that modifies `_campaign-state.yaml`
923	**When** the modification is about to be written
924	**Then** a `.bak` copy is written first (read-backup-modify-write pattern)
925	
926	**Given** the test suite at `test/test-skf-campaign-state.py`
927	**When** tests run via `uv run`
928	**Then** all state schema validation and backup behavior tests pass
929	
930	**Deliverables:**
931	- `src/skf-campaign/` directory with full structure
932	- `src/skf-campaign/SKILL.md` with trigger menu (`campaign`), mode routing, resume detection
933	- `src/skf-campaign/assets/campaign-state-schema.json`
934	- `test/test-skf-campaign-state.py`
935	
936	---
937	
938	### Story 4.2: Campaign setup + campaign-brief.yaml (Stage 0)
939	
940	As a campaign operator,
941	I want Stage 0 to create a campaign state file and brief from my inputs,
942	So that the campaign has a persistent starting point that survives context death.
943	
944	**Acceptance Criteria:**
945	
946	**Given** target libraries, tiers, and pins provided by the user
947	**When** the setup step runs
948	**Then** `_campaign-state.yaml` is created and validates against the schema
949	**And** `campaign-brief.yaml` is generated from `templates/campaign-brief-template.yaml`
950	
951	**Given** no explicit health_findings_queue configuration
952	**When** setup runs
953	**Then** health_findings_queue defaults to local (project-local queue)
954	**And** a one-time opt-in prompt is offered for the shared queue
955	
956	**Given** the generated state file
957	**When** validated against `assets/campaign-state-schema.json`
958	**Then** validation passes with no errors
959	
960	**Deliverables:**
961	- `src/skf-campaign/references/step-01-setup.md`
962	- `src/skf-campaign/templates/campaign-brief-template.yaml`

964	---
965	
966	### Story 4.3: Strategy generation + provenance (Stages 1 + 3)
967	
968	As a campaign operator,
969	I want a human-readable strategy view and verified source provenance,
970	So that I can review the plan before execution and trust that all targets are accessible.

972	**Acceptance Criteria:**

975	**When** the strategy step runs

978	**Given** a state file with targets
979	**When** the provenance step runs

982	**And** inaccessible repos produce a clear error and block the campaign

984	**Deliverables:**

989	
990	### Story 4.4: Pin validation (Stage 2)
991	
992	As a campaign operator,
993	I want version pins validated against actual releases before the campaign begins,
994	So that invalid pins are caught early with actionable suggestions.
995	
996	**Acceptance Criteria:**
997	
998	**Given** skills with valid version pins in the state file
999	**When** `campaign-validate-pins.py` runs
1000	**Then** all pins are confirmed against gh releases and package registries
1001	**And** the validation result is recorded in the state file
1002	
1003	**Given** a skill with an invalid version pin
1004	**When** validation runs
1005	**Then** a clear error is reported with suggested corrections (nearest valid versions)
1006	
1007	**Given** a deepwiki invocation with `--pin <version>`
1008	**When** pin validation runs
1009	**Then** the same `campaign-validate-pins.py` validator is used (shared infrastructure with FR-36)
1010	
1011	**Given** the test suite at `test/test-skf-campaign-pins.py`
1012	**When** tests run via `uv run`
1013	**Then** all pin validation tests pass
1014	
1015	**Deliverables:**
1016	- `src/skf-campaign/references/step-03-pins.md`
1017	- `src/skf-campaign/scripts/campaign-validate-pins.py`
1018	- `test/test-skf-campaign-pins.py`
1019	
1020	---
1021	
1022	### Story 4.5: Dependency tracking + enforcement
1023	
1024	As a campaign operator,
1025	I want skills with declared dependencies to execute in the correct order,
1026	So that no skill starts before its prerequisites have passed.
1027	
1028	**Acceptance Criteria:**
1029	
1030	**Given** skills with `depends_on` edges declared in the state file
1031	**When** the campaign computes execution order
1032	**Then** a topological sort determines the correct sequence
1033	**And** the execution_order is written to the state file's dependency_graph
1034	
1035	**Given** a circular dependency in the declared edges
1036	**When** the campaign attempts to compute execution order at setup time
1037	**Then** the circular dependency is detected and rejected with a clear error listing the cycle
1038	
1039	**Given** a skill whose dependency has not yet completed (status != completed)
1040	**When** the campaign attempts to start that skill
1041	**Then** the skill is blocked with a clear message naming the unmet dependency
1042	**And** a manual override flag is available to force execution
1043	
1044	---
1045	
1046	### Story 4.6: Per-skill loop + kickoff emitter (Stage 4)
1047	
1048	As a campaign operator,
1049	I want each skill processed in dependency order with a fresh-context kickoff message,
1050	So that every skill iteration starts with the right context regardless of session boundaries.
1051	
1052	**Acceptance Criteria:**
1053	
1054	**Given** 3 skills in computed execution order
1055	**When** the skill loop runs
1056	**Then** each skill is processed in order: pre-apply, kickoff emit, AN, BS, CS, TS, mark complete, propagate findings, doc-rot check
1057	
1058	**Given** a skill entering the loop
1059	**When** the kickoff emitter runs
1060	**Then** a kickoff message is generated from `templates/kickoff-template.md` containing: skill name, brief summary, dependency state, directive, workarounds applied
1061	
1062	**Given** a skill completing successfully
1063	**When** its state is updated
1064	**Then** the status transitions atomically from `active` to `completed`
1065	**And** `completed_at` timestamp and `quality_score` are recorded
1066	
1067	**Given** a skill that fails during processing
1068	**When** its state is updated
1069	**Then** the status transitions atomically from `active` to `failed`
1070	**And** downstream skills that do not depend on it continue processing
1071	
1072	**Deliverables:**
1073	- `src/skf-campaign/references/step-05-skill-loop.md`
1074	- `src/skf-campaign/templates/kickoff-template.md`
1075	
1076	---
1077	
1078	### Story 4.7: Tier B batch + capstone (Stages 5-6)
1079	
1080	As a campaign operator,
1081	I want Tier B skills batched through QS and a capstone stack skill composed from completed skills,
1082	So that lower-tier skills are handled efficiently and the final stack integrates everything.
1083	
1084	**Acceptance Criteria:**
1085	
1086	**Given** Tier B skills in the campaign state
1087	**When** the batch stage runs
1088	**Then** QS `--batch` is invoked for all Tier B skills
1089	
1090	**Given** completed individual skills from the skill loop and batch stages
1091	**When** the capstone stage runs
1092	**Then** SS compose-mode is invoked to produce a stack skill from the completed skills
1093	**And** the stack skill quality is recorded in the campaign state
1094	
1095	**Deliverables:**
1096	- `src/skf-campaign/references/step-06-batch.md`
1097	- `src/skf-campaign/references/step-07-capstone.md`
1098	
1099	---
1100	
1101	### Story 4.8: Verification + refinement (Stages 7-8)
1102	
1103	As a campaign operator,
1104	I want generated skills verified and refined before export,
1105	So that the final skill set meets quality standards with architecture-aware improvements.
1106	
1107	**Acceptance Criteria:**
1108	
1109	**Given** completed skills from the loop, batch, and capstone stages
1110	**When** the verification stage runs
1111	**Then** VS produces a verification report covering all generated skills
1112	
1113	**Given** a VS verification report
1114	**When** the refinement stage runs

1116	**And** refinement results are recorded in the campaign state

1118	**Deliverables:**

1122	---
1123	
1124	### Story 4.9: Export + maintenance + campaign report (Stages 9-10)
1125	
1126	As a campaign operator,
1127	I want a gated export with a comprehensive campaign report,
1128	So that no skills are exported without confirmation and the full campaign is auditable.
1129	
1130	**Acceptance Criteria:**
1131	
1132	**Given** all prior stages completed
1133	**When** the export stage runs
1134	**Then** a single write-gate HALT is presented requiring explicit user confirmation before any files are written
1135	
1136	**Given** all stages completed and export confirmed
1137	**When** the maintenance stage runs
1138	**Then** a campaign report is generated from `templates/campaign-report-template.md` containing: skills completed, quality scores per skill, findings summary, workarounds applied, doc-rot corrections, total duration
1139	**And** the shared health check is chained after report generation
1140	
1141	**Given** the campaign report
1142	**When** it is written
1143	**Then** it is placed at a predictable path in `forge-data/` and referenced from the campaign state
1144	
1145	**Deliverables:**
1146	- `src/skf-campaign/references/step-10-export.md`
1147	- `src/skf-campaign/references/step-11-maintenance.md`
1148	- `src/skf-campaign/scripts/campaign-report.py`
1149	- `src/skf-campaign/templates/campaign-report-template.md`
1150	
1151	---
1152	
1153	### Story 4.10: Campaign resume
1154	
1155	As a campaign operator,
1156	I want to resume a campaign from any skill after a session boundary,
1157	So that context death, multi-day runs, and interruptions do not lose progress.
1158	
1159	**Acceptance Criteria:**
1160	
1161	**Given** a campaign with 5 of 10 skills completed in `_campaign-state.yaml`
1162	**When** `campaign resume --from=skill-6` is invoked
1163	**Then** skills 1 through 5 are skipped (their completed status is preserved)
1164	**And** the named skill becomes the entry point for the skill loop
1165	
1166	**Given** a resume invocation
1167	**When** the state file is loaded
1168	**Then** state integrity is validated against the schema before resuming
1169	**And** the `.bak` file is checked for consistency
1170	
1171	**Given** a campaign that was interrupted mid-skill (status = active)
1172	**When** resume is invoked without `--from`
1173	**Then** the campaign resumes from the last active skill
1174	
1175	**Given** a campaign state file written days ago in a different session
1176	**When** resume is invoked
1177	**Then** the campaign resumes correctly (no in-memory state dependency per NFR-2)
1178	
1179	---
1180	
1181	### Story 4.11: Standing directive
1182	
1183	As a campaign operator,
1184	I want a file-based directive that the campaign reads at every stage transition,
1185	So that I can adjust campaign-wide policy without editing the state file or relying on LLM memory.
1186	
1187	**Acceptance Criteria:**
1188	
1189	**Given** a `_campaign-directive.md` file in the campaign working directory
1190	**When** the campaign transitions between stages
1191	**Then** the directive file is read and its contents applied to the current stage
1192	
1193	**Given** no `_campaign-directive.md` file present
1194	**When** the campaign transitions between stages
1195	**Then** the campaign proceeds with default behavior and no error
1196	
1197	**Given** a directive file
1198	**When** it is inspected
1199	**Then** it is a plain markdown file that is auditable and version-controllable
1200	**And** it is file-based with no memory dependency (per NFR-2)
1201	
1202	**Deliverables:**
1203	- `src/skf-campaign/references/campaign-directive-spec.md`
1204	
1205	---

1208
