  "content": "# Epic 3 Retrospective — Quality, Scale & Workaround Automation\n\n**Date:** 2026-05-27\n**Facilitator:** bmad-retrospective (Amelia)\n**Project Lead:** Armel\n**Epic status at retro:** all 6 stories `done`; epic `in-progress` (to be flipped `done`); retrospective key `epic-3-retrospective` flipped `optional` → `done`.\n**Previous retrospective:** epic-2-retro-2026-05-26.md (deepwiki: Zero-Ceremony Skill Creation).\n\n## Summary\n\nEpic 3 delivered quality gates, scale automation, and workaround infrastructure across 6 stories spanning two pipeline domains (TS and CS) plus one standalone shared module:\n\n| Story | Outcome | Review result |\n|-------|---------|---------------|\n| 3.1 Tiered quality gate — hard gate | `step-hard-gate.md` inserted between external-validators and score in TS chain. Binary pass/block on critical/high findings. Headless envelope emits exit code 2 on block. | report.md stepsCompleted chain fixed during dev (hardcoded list needed `'hard-gate'` added). All tests pass. |\n| 3.2 Per-pipeline quality thresholds | §1b lookup table in init.md (deepwiki: 90, forge: 80, forge-quick: 80, campaign: 90). 4-layer threshold precedence in score.md. Forger forwards `pipeline_alias`. | Code review fixed stale JSON template comment in score.md §3a. Cross-reference updates to SKILL.md, customize.toml, pipeline-contracts.md. |\n| 3.3 Evidence report on threshold fallback | §4b fallback logic in score.md. Evidence report at `{forge_version}/evidence-report-fallback.md`. Fallback surfaced in score.md §6/§7/§8, report.md §6/§4c, SKILL.md. | 0 critical, 1 medium (test count doc mismatch, auto-fixed). 46 tests, 196 adjacent tests verified zero regressions. |\n| 3.4 Auto-shard at 400-line ceiling | `step-auto-shard.md` (step 5b) in CS chain between doc-sources and validate. Tier 2 selective extraction, Tier 1 preservation, 5 §-prefixed sections. | Senior review: 0 CRITICAL, 0 HIGH, 1 MEDIUM (stale test counts in Completion Notes, auto-fixed), 2 LOW (documented). 1964 tests passed. |\n| 3.5 Pre-apply workaround registry | `_known-workarounds.yaml` shared seed (25 GMC fingerprints). `skf-preapply.py` with argparse, JSON stdout, merge logic, substring + regex matching. | Review added NoReturn annotation, re.error handling for invalid regex, removed dead importlib import. 39 tests. |\n| 3.6 Doc-rot correction hooks | `step-doc-rot.md` (step 5c) in CS chain between auto-shard and validate. 13 grep patterns across 7 categories. CORRECTION block annotation format. | Review fixed copy-paste class name, typo, stale chain target message, added direct chain target existence test. 2050 total tests passed. |\n\nDelivered on `quality-scale-and-workaround-automation` branch via 6 commits (`44753442` through `f00f4c63`). Total test count grew from 1766 (Epic 2 close) to 2050 (+284 tests, +16.1%).\n\n## What went well\n\n1. **Step chain insertion pattern is now muscle memory.** Stories 3.1, 3.4, and 3.6 each inserted a new step into an existing chain (TS and CS respectively). All three followed the same protocol: update upstream `nextStepFile`, create new step file with `nextStepFile` pointing downstream, verify the Stages table, grep for hardcoded chain references. Story 3.1 discovered report.md's hardcoded stepsCompleted — that discovery prevented the same class of bug in Stories 3.4 and 3.6 (which confirmed CS report.md had no such pattern). The pattern is validated and repeatable.\n\n2. **Structural integration tests scaled linearly with complexity.** Each story produced between 27 and 53 structural tests. The tests are pathlib-based, verify file existence, frontmatter content, step chain integrity, and section structure — they catch real regressions (Story 3.6 caught an auto-shard chain assertion that needed updating). Test count grew 16.1% across the epic with zero false positives or flaky tests.\n\n3. **Story intelligence carry-forward matured into a reliable mechanism.** Every story explicitly referenced learnings from prior stories in a \"Story N.M Learnings (Apply These)\" section within Dev Notes. The items were actionable and specific (e.g., \"Register test file in package.json\" appeared in every story after 3.1). No previously-identified pattern was missed in subsequent stories.\n\n4. **Two distinct pipeline domains touched cleanly.** Stories 3.1-3.3 modified the TS (Test Skill) pipeline while Stories 3.4 and 3.6 modified the CS (Create Skill) pipeline. Story 3.5 created a standalone shared module. Despite touching different codebases, all stories followed identical conventions: §-prefixed sections, frontmatter chaining, auto-proceed semantics, structural test pattern. The architectural consistency across SKF workflows is paying dividends.\n\n5. **Code reviews found real but non-critical issues.** Every story went through review. Findings were minor: stale comments, test count mismatches in completion notes, copy-paste class names, missing error handling annotations. Zero CRITICAL findings across the entire epic — a first. The codebase quality bar is rising.\n\n6. **Self-audit for internal references succeeded.** The Epic 2 commitment to grep for internal identifiers (story IDs, epic numbers) before review was applied in all 6 stories. Zero internal reference leakage findings in any review — down from 2 incidents in Epic 2. The habit is established.\n\n7. **Cross-platform safety conventions held.** `encoding=\"utf-8\"` file reads, `Path.as_posix()` in assertions, and PEP 723 headers were consistently applied in Story 3.5's Python script. No Windows CI failures related to cross-platform issues.\n\n## Patterns across all six stories\n\n### Step file convention is fully crystallized\n\nEvery step file created in Epic 3 follows an identical structure:\n- Frontmatter with `nextStepFile` (chaining)\n- `# Step Nb: Name` heading matching the Stages table\n- `## STEP GOAL:` heading\n- `## Rules` section (auto-proceed, no user interaction, graceful skip)\n- `## MANDATORY SEQUENCE` with §-prefixed subsections\n- Context logging variables for observability\n\nThis convention was established in Epics 1 and 2 but crystallized in Epic 3 into a truly mechanical pattern. Any future step file insertion can follow this template verbatim.\n\n### Threshold and quality gate separation is architecturally clean\n\nThe hard gate (severity-based, binary) and soft gate (threshold-based, scored) are completely independent:\n- Hard gate reads findings from upstream analysis — zero awareness of threshold values\n- Soft gate reads the threshold from a 4-layer resolution chain — zero awareness of finding severity\n- Evidence report bridges the two: fires only when the soft gate falls back, documents the findings that prevented the higher threshold\n\nThis separation means future changes to thresholds, scoring rules, or severity definitions can be made independently without cross-contamination.\n\n### Pre-apply and doc-rot hooks serve complementary roles\n\nPre-apply (Story 3.5) runs before pipeline iterations — proactively fixes known issues. Doc-rot hooks (Story 3.6) run post-compile — reactively annotates corrections found in feeder artifacts. Together they create a two-sided quality net: pre-apply prevents re-discovery of solved issues, doc-rot surfaces new corrections deterministically. Both use pattern matching (substring/regex) rather than AI judgment.\n\n### Test registration in package.json is a load-bearing checklist item\n\nEvery story explicitly noted: \"Register test file in package.json's test:python command.\" This is the one step that, if missed, leaves an entire test suite dormant in CI with zero warning. Epic 3 had 6 new test files and all were registered correctly. The pattern works because it's called out in every story's dev notes — not because it's obvious.\n\n## Challenges and growth areas\n\n1. **No empirical pipeline validation.** Like Epic 2, all stories were validated with structural tests (`npm test`), not by running the actual pipelines against real skills. The hard gate, per-pipeline thresholds, evidence report, auto-shard, pre-apply, and doc-rot hooks have never been exercised in a real forge/deepwiki run. The `[PENDING VALIDATION]` markers from Epic 2 carry forward, and Epic 3 adds its own implicit ones. The first real pipeline run will be the true test.\n\n2. **Evidence report fallback has a doc mismatch.** Story 3.3's story file says \"46 structural tests\" in the Completion Notes but \"27 structural integration tests\" in the File List section. The actual count is 27 tests in the file. The Completion Notes were corrected during review, but the inconsistency pattern (test counts drifting between story sections) appeared in Stories 3.3 and 3.4. This is a minor documentation hygiene issue but worth noting.\n\n3. **CS pipeline chain grew to 8 steps.** With the addition of step-auto-shard (5b) and step-doc-rot (5c), the CS compile-to-validate chain is now: compile → doc-sources → auto-shard → doc-rot → validate → generate-artifacts → report → health-check. While each step is well-bounded, the chain length means a single CS invocation traverses 8 step files. Monitoring for aggregate latency in headless mode will be important.\n\n4. **Shared seed registry is static.** The `_known-workarounds.yaml` seed was created with 25 GMC fingerprints but has no mechanism for automated growth. New workarounds discovered during pipeline runs are appended to project-local registries only. The seed can only grow via maintainer PRs. This is by design (architecture anti-pattern: never modify seed at runtime), but means the seed's value depends on periodic manual curation.\n\n## Previous retrospective follow-through\n\n**Epic 2 retro committed to 5 items (4 process + 1 deferred doc). Assessment:**\n\n1. ✅ **Self-audit for internal references before review.** Applied in all 6 stories. Zero internal reference findings in any Epic 3 review (down from 2 in Epic 2). Habit established.\n\n2. ✅ **Cross-reference grep when modifying step files.** Applied consistently. Story 3.1 found report.md hardcoded stepsCompleted (and fixed it). Story 3.4 confirmed CS report.md had no such pattern. Story 3.6 updated auto-shard test chain assertion. The grep-before-modify pattern caught real issues.\n\n3. ✅ **Continue \"Previous Story Intelligence\" convention.** All 6 stories included explicit \"Story N.M Learnings (Apply These)\" sections with actionable items from prior stories. Knowledge transfer is working.\n\n4. ✅ **Continue mock validation in test reviews.** No mock-related findings in Epic 3 — all tests are structural (pathlib/content-based) or functional (subprocess CLI). The pattern is holding.\n\n5. ⏳ **docs/skill-model.md metadata.json doc_sources.** Still deferred — appropriate. No skills have been compiled with doc_sources in production yet.\n\n**Score:** 4/5 completed, 1 appropriately deferred. All process commitments followed through.\n\n## Technical debt carried forward\n\n| Item | Source | Severity | Impact on Epic 4 |\n|------|--------|----------|-------------------|\n| No empirical pipeline validation | Epics 2+3 | Medium | Epic 4's skill-loop (step-05) invokes the full pipeline; first real test will happen there |\n| `[PENDING VALIDATION]` decomposition thresholds | Epic 2 Story 2.4 | Medium | None (decomposition is deepwiki-only; campaign doesn't use it directly) |\n| Evidence report test count inconsistency | Story 3.3 | Low | None (doc-only issue within story file) |\n| CS pipeline chain length (8 steps) | Cumulative | Low | Campaign's CS invocation traverses all 8; monitor latency |\n| Static shared seed registry | Story 3.5 | Low | Campaign step-05 uses pre-apply; seed effectiveness depends on GMC fingerprint relevance |\n| docs/skill-model.md doc_sources | Epic 1 carry-over | Low | None |\n\nNo debt items block Epic 4.\n\n## Epic 4 preview — Campaign Orchestration (v2.0)\n\n**Stories:** 4.1 Campaign workflow scaffolding + state schema · 4.2 Campaign setup + campaign-brief.yaml · 4.3 Strategy generation + provenance · 4.4 Pin validation · 4.5 Dependency tracking + enforcement · 4.6 Per-skill loop + kickoff emitter · 4.7 Tier B batch + capstone · 4.8 Verification + refinement · 4.9 Export + maintenance + campaign report · 4.10 Campaign resume · 4.11 Standing directive · 4.12 onboard removal + v2.0 documentation\n\n**Dependencies on Epic 3:**\n- Story 4.6 (per-skill loop) invokes the full pipeline including: hard gate (3.1), per-pipeline thresholds (3.2), evidence report (3.3), auto-shard (3.4), pre-apply (3.5), and doc-rot hooks (3.6). All Epic 3 features are consumed by campaign's skill-loop stage.\n- Story 4.4 (pin validation) uses `campaign-validate-pins.py` which shares infrastructure with Story 2.7's `skf-validate-pins.py` — no Epic 3 dependency.\n- Story 4.12 removes the `onboard` alias — depends on Epic 2 (deprecation notice), not Epic 3.\n\n**Preparation needs:**\n- Story 4.1 creates a new workflow directory (`src/skf-campaign/`) — first greenfield workflow since Epic 1. Follows established patterns (SKILL.md, manifest.yaml, references/, assets/, templates/, scripts/).\n- Campaign state schema (`_campaign-state.yaml`) introduces YAML-based persistent state — a new pattern for SKF. Step files will follow read-backup-modify-write discipline per NFR-2.\n- Story 4.6 is the integration capstone: per-skill pre-apply → kickoff → AN → BS → CS → TS → mark complete → propagate findings → doc-rot check. This is the first real end-to-end pipeline execution and will validate all Epic 3 features empirically.\n\n**No significant discoveries** from Epic 3 that invalidate Epic 4's current plan. Quality gates, scale automation, and workaround infrastructure are all additive and transparent to the campaign orchestrator.\n\n## Commitments\n\n### Process improvements (carry into Epic 4+)\n\n1. **Continue step chain insertion protocol.** Update upstream nextStepFile, create step with downstream nextStepFile, update Stages table, grep for hardcoded chain references. Validated 3-for-3 in Epic 3.\n\n2. **Continue structural integration test pattern.** pathlib-based file existence, frontmatter checks, content regex matching, chain integrity verification. 284 new tests in Epic 3 with zero flaky tests.\n\n3. **Continue story intelligence carry-forward.** Every story includes \"Previous Story Learnings (Apply These)\" section with actionable items.\n\n4. **Continue self-audit for internal references.** Zero incidents in Epic 3 (down from 2 in Epic 2). Keep applying.\n\n5. **Continue cross-reference grep on step file modifications.** Caught real issues in 3 out of 6 stories.\n\n6. **Register every new test file in package.json test:python.** Called out explicitly in every story's dev notes — maintain this.\n\n7. **NEW: Verify test counts match across story file sections.** Stories 3.3 and 3.4 both had test count inconsistencies between Completion Notes and File List sections. Dev agent should reconcile counts before marking story done.\n\n### Documentation updates needed\n\n8. `docs/workflows.md` — should reference quality gate architecture (hard gate + soft gate + evidence report) in the TS workflow description. Currently lacks Epic 3 feature documentation.\n\n9. `docs/skill-model.md` metadata.json doc_sources — deferred (carried from Epic 1 retro). Still appropriate to defer.\n\n### Standing flags (no fire date)\n\n10. **Empirical pipeline validation.** The first real pipeline run (deepwiki or forge against a real repo) will be the validation event for all Epic 1-3 features. Campaign Story 4.6 is the most likely trigger. Track outcomes.\n\n11. **Shared seed registry curation.** `_known-workarounds.yaml` starts at 25 fingerprints. Monitor effectiveness during campaign runs. Add fingerprints via maintainer PRs as new patterns emerge.\n\n## Readiness assessment\n\n| Dimension | Status |\n|-----------|--------|\n| Story completion | 6/6 done |\n| Test count | 2050 total passing (+284 from Epic 2, +16.1%) |\n| New step files | 3 (step-hard-gate.md in TS, step-auto-shard.md + step-doc-rot.md in CS) |\n| New shared scripts | 1 (skf-preapply.py + _known-workarounds.yaml seed registry) |\n| Code review | All 6 stories reviewed; 0 CRITICAL, 0 HIGH, ~5 MEDIUM, ~5 LOW across epic |\n| TS pipeline chain | Intact: init → detect-mode → coverage-check → coherence-check → external-validators → step-hard-gate → score → report → health-check |\n| CS pipeline chain | Intact: compile → step-doc-sources → step-auto-shard → step-doc-rot → validate → generate-artifacts → report → health-check |\n| Backward compatibility | All interactive paths, existing pipeline aliases, standalone TS/CS invocations verified per story |\n| Architecture compliance | All implementations match architecture spec (FR-11 through FR-19) |\n| Epic 4 dependencies | Quality gates in TS, scale automation in CS, pre-apply shared module — all ready for campaign consumption |\n| Unresolved blockers | None |\n| Deferred work tracked | 6 items, all low-medium severity, none blocking Epic 4 |\n\nEpic 3 is complete. Quality gates (hard gate + soft gate + evidence report), scale automation (auto-shard), workaround infrastructure (pre-apply registry), and doc-rot correction hooks are all in place. Epic 4 (Campaign Orchestration) can start on this branch or after merge to `main`.\n\n## Next steps\n\n1. Flip epic-3 status from `in-progress` to `done` in sprint-status.yaml.\n2. Flip epic-3-retrospective from `optional` to `done` in sprint-status.yaml.\n3. Open PR from `quality-scale-and-workaround-automation` → `main` when ready.\n4. Begin Epic 4 with Story 4.1 (Campaign workflow scaffolding + state schema) — first greenfield workflow directory.\n5. No epic-update review session needed — nothing in Epic 3 invalidates Epic 4's current definitions.\n"

#   - done: Story completed

# Retrospective Status:
#   - optional: Can be completed but not required

140	**Purpose:** Drift detection between skill and current source.
141	
142	**When to Use:** To check if a skill has fallen out of date with its source code. Works for both individual skills and stack skills.
143	
144	**Key Steps:** Load skill → Re-index source → Structural diff (incl. script/asset drift) → Semantic diff (Deep) → Classify severity → Doc drift → Report
145	
146	**Stack skill support:** Code-mode stacks are audited per-library against their sources. Compose-mode stacks check constituent freshness via metadata hash comparison — if a constituent skill was updated after the stack was composed, audit flags it as constituent drift. Stack skills that need updating are redirected to `@Ferris SS` for re-composition (surgical update is not supported for stacks).
147	
148	**Agent:** Ferris (Audit mode)
149	
150	---
151	
152	### Test Skill (TS)
153	
154	**Command:** `@Ferris TS`
155	
156	**Purpose:** Verifies whether a skill covers its target completely and accurately. Naive and contextual modes. Quality gate before export.
157	
158	**When to Use:** After creating or updating a skill, before exporting.
159	
160	**Key Steps:** Load skill → Detect mode → Coverage check → Coherence check → External validation (skill-check, tessl) → Score → Gap report
161	
162	**Scored Categories:** Export Coverage (36%), Signature Accuracy (22%), Type Coverage (14%), Coherence (18%), External Validation (10%). Default pass threshold: **80%**. Pass routes to Export Skill; fail routes to Update Skill with a gap report. See [Completeness Scoring](../verifying-a-skill/#how-the-score-is-computed) for the full formula and tier adjustments.
163	
164	**Agent:** Ferris (Audit mode)
165	
166	---
167	
168	## Architecture Verification Workflows
169	
170	### Verify Stack (VS)
171	
172	**Command:** `@Ferris VS`
173	
174	**Purpose:** Pre-code stack feasibility verification. Cross-references generated skills against architecture and PRD documents with three passes: coverage, integration compatibility, and requirements.
175	
176	**When to Use:** After generating individual skills with CS/QS, before building a stack skill — to verify the tech stack can support the architecture.
177	
178	**Key Steps:** Load skills + docs → Coverage analysis → Integration verification → Requirements check → Synthesize verdict → Present report
179	
180	**Agent:** Ferris (Audit mode)
181	
182	---
183	
184	### Refine Architecture (RA)
185	
186	**Command:** `@Ferris RA`
187	
188	**Purpose:** Improves an architecture document using verified skill data as evidence. Takes the original architecture doc + generated skills + optional VS report, fills gaps, flags contradictions, and suggests improvements — all citing specific APIs.
189

id: toolu_01FXYperiabvJjhd4G1DHTMa
```
1	---
2	title: Verifying a Skill
3	description: How to audit any SKF-compiled skill — walk every instruction back to an upstream commit and line number in under 60 seconds.
4	---
5	
6	**Nothing is made up.** Every instruction in every skill traces back to a specific file, a specific line, and a specific commit in the upstream source. If a skill claims a function exists, you can open the real source tree at the pinned commit and see it with your own eyes. If the claim and the source disagree — that's a bug, and SKF treats it as one.
7	
8	---
9	
10	## The three-step audit
11	
12	Pick any symbol in any SKF-compiled skill. You can trace it to the exact line of upstream source in under 60 seconds.
13	
14	### 1. Open the skill's `metadata.json`
15	
16	Every skill ships a `metadata.json` next to its `SKILL.md`. Note two fields:
17	
18	- `source_commit` — the exact commit SHA the skill was compiled from
19	- `source_repo` — the upstream repository
20	
21	This is the anchor. Everything else traces back to this commit.
22	
23	### 2. Open the skill's `provenance-map.json`
24	
25	Provenance maps live in `forge-data/{skill}/{version}/provenance-map.json` alongside each compiled skill. Find your symbol. Every entry carries its own `source_file` and `source_line`:
26	
27	```json
28	{
29	  "export_name": "search",
30	  "export_type": "function",
31	  "params": ["query_text: str", "query_type: SearchType = GRAPH_COMPLETION", "top_k: int = 10"],
32	  "return_type": "List[SearchResult]",
33	  "source_file": "cognee/api/v1/search/search.py",
34	  "source_line": 27,
35	  "confidence": "T1",
36	  "extraction_method": "ast-grep"
37	}
38	```
39	
40	The snippet above is a real entry from [`forge-data/oms-cognee/1.0.0/provenance-map.json`](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/oh-my-skills/blob/main/forge-data/oms-cognee/1.0.0/provenance-map.json). Line number is not rounded. Confidence tier is explicit. Extraction method is named. Nothing is paraphrased.
41	
42	### 3. Visit the upstream repo at the pinned commit
43	
44	Open `{source_repo}` at `{source_commit}`, jump to `{source_file}` line `{source_line}`. The signature in `SKILL.md` should match what you see in the source.
45	
46	If it doesn't, **that's a bug**. [Open an issue](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/issues/new/choose). SKF will republish the skill with a new commit SHA and a new provenance map. Falsifiability isn't a feature — it's the whole deal.
47	
48	### Workflow-time enforcement
49	
50	The same anchor is enforced automatically by `skf-test-skill` and by gap-driven `skf-update-skill`. Before either workflow reads source at a recorded `source_line`, it runs `git rev-parse HEAD` on the local workspace and compares it to `metadata.source_commit`. If the workspace has drifted, the workflow halts with a `halted-for-workspace-drift` status and tells you the exact `git checkout {source_ref}` to re-sync — so spot-checks can never silently verify against the wrong tree. Pass `--allow-workspace-drift` to opt in to reading the current HEAD anyway; the override is recorded in the final report rather than hidden.
51	
52	---
53	
54	## Where to look for what
55	
56	Every file in the per-skill output carries a specific job. Here's the lookup table for the really skeptical:
57	
58	| Question | File |
59	|---|---|
60	| What commit was the source pinned to? | `skills/{name}/{version}/{name}/metadata.json` → `source_commit` |
61	| Which symbols are documented and where did each come from? | `forge-data/{name}/{version}/provenance-map.json` |
62	| What AST patterns were used for extraction? | `forge-data/{name}/{version}/extraction-rules.yaml` |
63	| What signatures, types, and examples did the extractor actually capture? | `forge-data/{name}/{version}/evidence-report.md` |
64	| How was the skill scored? Show me the math. | `forge-data/{name}/{version}/test-report-{name}.md` |
65	| How was the skill scoped, and what was deliberately left out? | `forge-data/{name}/skill-brief.yaml` |
66	
67	Everything a reader needs to reconstruct the compilation is in the two sibling directories: `skills/` ships to consumers, `forge-data/` is the audit trail.
68	
69	---
70	
71	## The scores, including the ones we lose
72	
73	Completeness scoring is never 100%. The [scoring formula](#how-the-score-is-computed) is deterministic and the pass threshold is **80%** — but every test report also logs the specific edges where a skill falls short, so the numbers aren't marketing.
74	
75	Take oh-my-skills' four reference skills as an example. Their scores range from **99.0% to 99.49%** — none are perfect, and every test report names the specific drift it found:
76	
77	| Skill | Score | What the report discloses |
78	|---|---|---|
79	| [oms-cocoindex](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/oh-my-skills/blob/main/forge-data/oms-cocoindex/0.3.37/test-report-oms-cocoindex.md) | **99.0%** | 114/114 provenance entries; 55 public-API denominator from `__init__.py` `__all__`; 20/20 sampled signatures matched. Two denominators (barrel vs. full surface) both disclosed with rationale. |
80	| [oms-cognee](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/oh-my-skills/blob/main/forge-data/oms-cognee/1.0.0/test-report-oms-cognee.md) | **99.0%** | 34/34 exports documented; denominator is the `cognee/__init__.py` barrel (61 lines, 34 public re-exports) at pinned commit `3c048aa4` (v1.0.0). |
81	| [oms-storybook-react-vite](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/oh-my-skills/blob/main/forge-data/oms-storybook-react-vite/10.3.5/test-report-oms-storybook-react-vite.md) | **99.49%** | 215/216 documented — the missing 1 entry is logged openly as **GAP-004**, a canonical surface count drift from the stated denominator. |
82	| [oms-uitripled](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/oh-my-skills/blob/main/forge-data/oms-uitripled/0.1.0/test-report-oms-uitripled.md) | **99.45%** | 34-entry denominator (not 11, not 25) with the full reconciliation reasoning in the report. |
83	
84	Perfection is suspicious. Visible fallibility is trustworthy. SKF writes down the edges it can't score cleanly — so you can read them and decide for yourself whether the remaining coverage is enough for your use case.
85	
86	### GAP-004: a worked example of the 1% that fails
87	
88	The [`oms-storybook-react-vite` test report](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/oh-my-skills/blob/main/forge-data/oms-storybook-react-vite/10.3.5/test-report-oms-storybook-react-vite.md) scores **215/216** — not 216/216. The missing 1 entry is logged as **GAP-004**: a canonical export surface count (via the provenance map) diverges from the stated denominator in metadata.json. The report names the gap, shows the math, and leaves the drift visible for the next recompilation pass. Nothing was hidden.
89	
90	That's the pattern SKF asks you to trust: when scoring can't reach 100%, the report says so, cites the line, and leaves a fingerprint for the next audit.
91	
92	---
93	
94	## How the Score Is Computed
95	
96	The Test Skill workflow (`@Ferris TS`) calculates the completeness score — a weighted measure of how thoroughly and accurately a skill documents its target. This score is the quality gate: pass and the skill is ready for export; fail and it routes to update-skill for remediation.
97	
98	### Categories and weights
99	
100	The score is the weighted sum of five categories:
101	
102	| Category | Weight | What it measures |
103	|---|---|---|
104	| **Export Coverage** | 36% | Percentage of source exports documented in `SKILL.md` |
105	| **Signature Accuracy** | 22% | Documented function signatures match actual source signatures (parameter names, types, order, return types) |
106	| **Type Coverage** | 14% | Types and interfaces referenced in exports are fully documented |
107	| **Coherence** | 18% | Cross-references resolve, integration patterns are complete (contextual mode only) |
108	| **External Validation** | 10% | Average of skill-check quality score (0–100) and tessl content score (0–100%) |
109	
110	### Formula
111	
112	```
113	total_score = sum(category_weight × category_score)
114	```
115	
116	Each category score is a percentage: `(items_passing / items_total) × 100`.
117	
118	**Coherence** (contextual mode) combines two sub-scores:
119	
120	```
121	coherence = (reference_validity × 0.6) + (integration_completeness × 0.4)
122	```
123	
124	If no integration patterns exist, coherence equals reference validity alone.
125	
126	**External validation** averages the two tools when both are available. When only one tool is available, that tool's score is used. When neither is available, the 10% weight is redistributed proportionally to the other active categories.
127	
128	### Deterministic scoring
129	
130	The weight redistribution and score aggregation are computed by a deterministic Python script ([`compute-score.py`](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/bmad-module-skill-forge/blob/main/src/skf-test-skill/scripts/compute-score.py)). The LLM extracts category scores from the test report, constructs a JSON input, invokes the script, and uses its output for the final score. Same inputs always produce the same score. If the script is unavailable, the LLM falls back to manual calculation using the same formulas.
131	
132	### Naive vs contextual mode
133	
134	Test Skill runs in one of two modes, detected automatically:
135	
136	- **Contextual mode** (stack skills) — all five categories scored with the default weights above.
137	- **Naive mode** (individual skills) — Coherence is not scored. Its 18% weight is redistributed:
138	
139	| Category | Naive Weight |
140	|---|---|
141	| Export Coverage | 45% |
142	| Signature Accuracy | 25% |
143	| Type Coverage | 20% |
144	| External Validation | 10% |
145	
146	### Tier adjustments
147	
148	Your forge tier determines which categories can be scored:
149	
150	| Tier | Skipped Categories | Reason |
151	|---|---|---|
152	| **Quick** | Signature Accuracy, Type Coverage | No AST parsing available |
153	| **Docs-only** | Signature Accuracy, Type Coverage | No source code to compare against |
154	| **Provenance-map** (State 2) | Signature Accuracy, Type Coverage | String comparison only, no semantic AST verification |
155	| **Forge / Forge+ / Deep** | None | Full AST-backed scoring |
156	
157	When categories are skipped, their combined weight is redistributed proportionally to the remaining active categories. A Quick-tier skill and a Deep-tier skill both pass at the same 80% threshold — the score reflects what your tier can actually measure.
158	
159	### Pass/fail
160	
161	```
162	threshold = custom_threshold OR 80% (default)
163	
164	score >= threshold  →  PASS  →  Recommend export-skill
165	score <  threshold  →  FAIL  →  Recommend update-skill
166	```
167	
168	The default is 80%. You can override it by specifying a custom threshold when invoking the workflow (e.g., "test this skill with a 70% threshold").
169	
170	### Gap severities
171	
172	When the score is calculated, each finding is classified by severity to guide remediation:
173	
174	| Severity | Examples |
175	|---|---|
176	| **Critical** | Missing exported function/class documentation |
177	| **High** | Signature mismatch between source and `SKILL.md` |
178	| **Medium** | Missing type/interface documentation; scripts/assets directory inconsistencies |
179	| **Low** | Missing optional metadata or examples; description optimization opportunities |
180	| **Info** | Style suggestions; discovery testing recommendations |
181	
182	### Score report output
183	
184	The test report includes a score breakdown table showing each category's raw score, weight, and weighted contribution:
185	
186	| Category | Score | Weight | Weighted |
187	|---|---|---|---|
188	| Export Coverage | 92% | 36% | 33.1% |
189	| Signature Accuracy | 85% | 22% | 18.7% |
190	| Type Coverage | 100% | 14% | 14.0% |
191	| Coherence | 80% | 18% | 14.4% |
192	| External Validation | 78% | 10% | 7.8% |
193	| **Total** | | **100%** | **88.0%** |
194	
195	The report also records `analysisConfidence` (full, provenance-map, metadata-only, remote-only, or docs-only) and includes a degradation notice when source access was limited.
196	
197	---
198	
199	## Build-time drift detection (for docs themselves)
200	
201	The SKF docs you're reading right now are themselves verified against oh-my-skills. A `docs/_data/pinned.yaml` anchor file records the exact version, commit SHA, and confidence tier of every reference skill. A Node validator (`tools/validate-docs-drift.js`) runs as part of `npm run quality` and:
202	
203	1. **Confirms canonical truth** — every anchor in `pinned.yaml` is cross-checked against the actual `metadata.json` in oh-my-skills. Version, commit, tier, and authority must match.
204	2. **Scans docs for stale prose** — every `.md` file is grepped for `<library> v?<x.y.z>` patterns and any version that disagrees with `pinned.yaml` is flagged with file + line number.
205	
206	If the validator flags drift, the CI fails before the docs get merged. It's the same "nothing is made up" contract SKF applies to skills, applied to the docs that describe SKF. When the anchor file is updated to reflect a new oh-my-skills release, the prose must update too — otherwise `npm run docs:validate-drift` blocks the merge.
207	
208	You can run it yourself from the SKF repo root:
209	
210	```bash
211	npm run docs:validate-drift
212	```
213	
214	Or point it at a different local copy of oh-my-skills:
215	
216	```bash
217	OMS=/path/to/your/oh-my-skills npm run docs:validate-drift
218	```
219	
220	Clean output looks like this:
221	
222	```
223	OK: 4 skills checked against /home/you/oh-my-skills, no drift.
224	```
225	
226	Dirty output cites exact file:line locations so the fix is mechanical.
227	
228	---
229	
230	## Reference output: oh-my-skills
231	
232	Every example in this page points at [**oh-my-skills**](https://github.com/armelhbobdad/oh-my-skills), the SKF reference portfolio. Four Deep-tier skills (cocoindex, cognee, Storybook v10, uitripled), each shipping its full audit trail alongside the compiled skill. Both the worked example for this page and the continuing proof that the pipeline does what it says. If you want to see what SKF produces when you run it on real libraries, that's the answer.
233	
```

102	---
103	
104	### Stack Skill (SS)
105	
106	**Command:** `@Ferris SS`
107	
108	**Purpose:** Consolidated project stack skill with integration patterns. Supports two modes: **code-mode** (analyzes a codebase) and **compose-mode** (synthesizes from existing skills + architecture document, no codebase required).
109	
110	**When to Use:** When you want your agent to understand your entire project stack — not just individual libraries. Use code-mode for existing projects; compose-mode activates automatically after the VS → RA verification path when skills exist but no codebase is present.
111	
112	**Key Steps (code-mode):** Detect manifests → Rank dependencies → Scope confirmation → Parallel extract → Detect integrations → Compile stack → Generate references
113	
114	**Key Steps (compose-mode):** Load existing skills → Confirm scope → Detect integrations from architecture doc → Compile stack → Generate references
115	
116	**Agent:** Ferris (Architect mode)
117	
118	---
119	
120	### Analyze Source (AN)
121	
122	**Command:** `@Ferris AN`
123	
124	**Purpose:** Decomposes a repo to discover what's worth skilling, and recommends a stack skill.
125	
126	**When to Use:** Brownfield onboarding of large repos or multi-service projects.
127	
128	**Key Steps:** Init → Scan project → Identify units → Map exports & detect integrations → Recommend → Generate briefs
129	
130	**Note:** Supports resume — if the session is interrupted mid-analysis, re-run `@Ferris AN` and Ferris will resume from where it left off.
131	
132	**Agent:** Ferris (Architect mode)
133	
134	---
135	
136	### Audit Skill (AS)

47	### Create Skill (CS)
48	
49	**Command:** `@Ferris CS`
50	
51	**Purpose:** Compile a skill from a brief. Supports `--batch` for multiple briefs.
52	
53	**When to Use:** After Brief Skill, or with an existing skill-brief.yaml.
54	
55	**Key Steps:** Load brief → Ecosystem check → Extract (AST + scripts/assets) → QMD enrich (Deep) → Compile → Doc sources → Validate → Generate
56	
57	**Agent:** Ferris (Architect mode)
58	
59	---
60	
61	### Update Skill (US)
62	
63	**Command:** `@Ferris US`
64	
65	**Purpose:** Regenerates the skill while preserving `[MANUAL]` sections. Detects individual vs stack internally.
66	
67	**When to Use:** After source code changes when an existing skill needs updating.
68	
69	**Key Steps:** Load existing → Detect changes (incl. scripts/assets) → Re-extract → Merge (preserve MANUAL) → Validate → Write → Report
70	
71	**Agent:** Ferris (Surgeon mode)
72	
73	---
74	
75	## Feature Workflows
76	
77	### Quick Skill (QS)
78	
79	**Command:** `@Ferris QS <package-or-url>` or `@Ferris QS <package-or-url>@<version>`
80	
81	**Purpose:** Brief-less fast skill with package-to-repo resolution.
82	
83	**When to Use:** When you need a skill quickly — no brief needed. Accepts package names or GitHub URLs. Append `@version` to target a specific version (e.g., `@Ferris QS cognee@1.0.0`).
84	
85	**Key Steps:** Resolve target → Ecosystem check → Quick extract → Compile → Validate → Write
86	
87	**Headless / batch flags:**
88	
89	- `--headless` / `-H` — auto-proceed all confirmation gates with documented defaults; emits structured stderr progress events and exit codes (see [Headless Mode](#headless-mode))
90	- `--batch <file>` — process N targets from a text file in sequence (one target per line, `#` comments and `language=<lang>` / `scope=<path>` per-line modifiers supported); implies `--headless`
91	- `--fail-fast` — only with `--batch`; abort the whole batch on the first per-target failure instead of recording it and proceeding
92	
93	**Per-target overrides** (apply to a single-target run, or globally to every target in `--batch`):
94	
95	- `--description "<string>"` — override the LLM-derived description used in `SKILL.md` frontmatter and `metadata.json`
96	- `--exports "name1,name2,..."` — override the extracted export list (comma-separated)
```

  "old_string": "### Pass/fail\n\n```\nthreshold = custom_threshold OR 80% (default)\n\nscore >= threshold  →  PASS  →  Recommend export-skill\nscore <  threshold  →  FAIL  →  Recommend update-skill\n```\n\nThe default is 80%. You can override it by specifying a custom threshold when invoking the workflow (e.g., \"test this skill with a 70% threshold\").",
  "new_string": "### Hard gate\n\nBefore scoring, a hard gate scans all findings for **Critical** and **High** severity. If any are present, the pipeline blocks immediately — no score is computed and the skill cannot pass regardless of coverage percentage. Medium, Low, and Info findings pass through to scoring.\n\n### Pass/fail\n\n```\nthreshold = pipeline_default OR custom_threshold OR 80% (default)\n\nscore >= threshold  →  PASS  →  Recommend export-skill\nscore <  threshold  →  FAIL  →  Recommend update-skill\n```\n\nThe default threshold is **80%**. Pipeline aliases declare their own defaults: `deepwiki` targets **90%**, `forge` and `forge-quick` target **80%**. You can override any default with a custom threshold (e.g., `TS[min:85]`).\n\nWhen a skill scores between 80% and its target threshold (e.g., 82% against a 90% target), the soft gate falls back to the 80% floor — the skill passes, but an evidence report is written to `forge-data/{skill}/{version}/evidence-report-fallback.md` documenting the quality compromise."
}
